# Tesseract project conversion

`tsrct-conv` converts Premiere timelines to editable FX documents and converts
editable FX documents to new Premiere projects. After Effects has best-effort
editable import and experimental native export; the
[AE format table](../../docs/formats/after-effects.md) records the current subset
and direction-specific proof. It preserves original Premiere media without transcoding and uses the
existing editable FX document and archive APIs. Project archives use the `.tsrct` extension.
Premiere import also retains the measured static Film Impact
[Pop entrance](../../docs/formats/premiere.md#measured-default-film-impact-pop-geometry)
and [neutral Stroke profiles](../../docs/formats/premiere.md#measured-neutral-film-impact-stroke-profiles)
as editable geometry. [Irregular source presentation](../../docs/formats/premiere.md#irregular-presentation-timing--import-only-approximation)
has bounded, warned admission with unit playback. These import-only subsets carry
explicit approximation and native-proof limits in the accuracy ledger below.


## Explicit Premiere media relocation

`convert source.prproj --to tesseract --sequence GUID --media-relink bindings.json
--output converted` explicitly binds original-project bytes and selected sequence
plus each supplied Media UID/authored `FilePath` to an absolute local file/hash.
This separate input runs before native path admission; it does not guess names,
rewrite the project, transcode or bypass media validation. Bindings are checked
again before publication. Defaults and unbound records are unchanged. The manifest
and typed APIs are documented in the [Premiere crate](../../crates/premiere_file/README.md).
Caller authority does not itself prove Adobe provenance or visual fidelity.
`--media-relink` is Premiere-import-only and cannot combine with `--media-map`.

## Explicit media preparation

`inspect INPUT [--composition ID | --sequence ID]` checks reached audio/video.
The default AE text shows two tables: compositions (ID, media check, size,
FPS, length, direct layers and layers including precomps) and deduplicated media
issues (status, source ID, affected composition IDs and source name). `OK` is
only a media-admission check, not a render guarantee; `TRANSCODE CANDIDATE`
requires a separate compatibility/preservation check before preparation.
`PATH UNRESOLVED` means no file was selected at the supported paths, not that no
copy exists anywhere. AE also checks the exact adjacent Collect Files layout
from native project folders; found unsupported codecs become preparation issues.
Use `--json` for the complete version-2 inventory (including paths and per-target
media), or `--metadata-only` for the original version-1, no-media-I/O inventory.
Inspect can complete successfully while reporting blocked media; normal import/Check
fails on used unsupported video.

`transcode input.mov --output output.mov --backend library|external-ffmpeg-command`
converts **one media file to one media file**, without reading a project or writing
a media map. Each invocation is independent: a failed file does not remove an
already successful output. Map assembly and project import with `--media-map`
are separate caller operations. Neither import nor inspect launches FFmpeg.
See [media preparation](../../docs/media-preparation.md) for target selection,
backend setup, quality/GOP/alpha/audio restrictions, cancellation, source binding,
SWF classification and evidence limitations.

The app contains CLI setup, a static format registry, typed option adapters, and
common execution/result presentation. The `premiere_file` and `aftereffects_file`
libraries own conversion, validation, staging, and publication through the
[`fx_conv` contract](../../crates/fx_conv/README.md). Each format registers its
extensions and optional import/export routes; the CLI does not maintain a matrix
of format-pair implementations. Ordinary Premiere conversion coordinates the
existing native and AE staging/import APIs when editable linked content is needed.
Native-supported content stays in Premiere; neither format library depends on the
other, and the neutral report carries no Adobe-specific identity. No manual layer
selection or extra hybrid flag is required. Native-to-native conversion is not an
implicit chain.
After Effects import produces an editable `project.tsrct`; experimental export
produces `project.aep`. Neither route establishes render fidelity. See the
[AE support and approximation ledger](../../docs/after-effects-support.md).
The former private source-to-comparison guide is no longer distributed;
independent render proof remains a separate, authorized step.

The conversion boundary is one typed Premiere sequence and one editable FX
document. `PrProjectFile` is the shared Premiere model used by both reader and
writer. Library coordination resolves media, checks source identity, assigns
package paths, and publishes the files.
The CLI accepts standalone documents, not full application web projects. A web project
can contain additional compositions, captions and tracks, so converting one would
require an explicit adapter that accounts for all content and rejects unsupported
structures. Selecting only its first composition would lose edits.

## Build and run

From this repository's root:

```sh
make build
target/debug/tsrct-conv inspect project.prproj --json
target/debug/tsrct-conv convert project.prproj --to tesseract --sequence 'GUID-from-inspect' --output tesseract_output
target/debug/tsrct-conv convert tesseract_output/project.tsrct --to premiere --output premiere_output
target/debug/tsrct-conv inspect source.aep
target/debug/tsrct-conv convert source.aep --to tesseract --output after_effects_output --composition 1
target/debug/tsrct-conv convert after_effects_output/project.tsrct --to after-effects --output native_output --check
```

`--fps` sets the frame rate of an export from Tesseract; the import directions
reject it. Premiere export accepts 23.976, 24, 25, 29.97, 30, 50, 59.94 or 60
(default 30). Linked AEP scopes support exact 24, 25, 30, 50 and 60 fps.
Fractional sequence rates retain native Premiere output with diagnostics instead
of changing cadence to fit an AEP clock. After Effects export accepts any rate
up to 240 (default 24).

AEP → Tesseract also accepts `--available-fonts fonts.json`, an explicit JSON
array of available PostScript face names. Fonts absent from this inventory become
a same-style Inter face when declared, otherwise Inter-Regular, with a warning
naming the original font and the substitution. Omit the option to preserve the
previous font behavior; an empty array explicitly declares no available faces.
This does not discover, download or package font bytes—stage/import them before
rendering. The option is rejected for other routes. See the
[font policy and limitations](../../crates/aftereffects_file/README.md#explicit-font-availability-on-aep-import).

Tesseract exports to After Effects and Premiere sample FX JsScript for direct
or linked AEP content at 4× the output FPS on an integer-millisecond rounded
grid; native Premiere preparation is unchanged. At 30fps this is approximately
120Hz. Subframe pulses, topology changes and motion blur can be missed; sampled
scalar tracks use Linear/Hold interpolation and may differ between samples.
Both `--check` and Write use this preparation policy.

The binary is under `$CARGO_TARGET_DIR/debug` when that variable is set. The
output parent must exist; the destination must be new. Either direction accepts
`--check` to validate media and conversion requirements without publishing output
(temporary staging may be used and cleaned):

```sh
target/debug/tsrct-conv convert project.prproj --to tesseract --output tesseract_output --check
target/debug/tsrct-conv convert project.tsrct --to premiere --output premiere_output --check
```

`convert` accepts the source path as its sole positional argument, and `--to` is required.
Running `tsrct-conv` without arguments prints help.

`convert` and `transcode` report progress on stderr about every five seconds,
including a heartbeat while preparing or waiting without new measurements. Short
commands normally finish before the first heartbeat. In all four conversion
directions, measured phases report processed/total tracks, clips or layers,
percentage and an estimated **phase** remaining time based on observed throughput.
These are phase-local estimates, not whole-command percentages or promises of
fidelity. Root-layer/root-clip units include their nested work; a single complex
unit can take longer than others, so estimates may change as measured throughput
changes. Consecutive hybrid scopes reset their own measurements. `--check` uses
the same conversion measurements without publishing output.

Reading, media preparation, serialization and publication identify their current
phase without inventing a percentage or ETA when no denominator is available.
A counted phase initially says `estimating`; an estimate becomes unavailable if
work has not advanced for ten seconds. A heartbeat never counts down an old ETA.
Transcode continues to report measured media time and encoding ETA.

Use `convert ... --json` or `transcode ... --json` for one final JSON result on
stdout and NDJSON events on stderr. Events carry `schemaVersion: 1`, `type`
(`progress`, `diagnostic`, `complete`, or `error`), and `command`. Progress adds
`phase`, `elapsedSeconds`, `completed`, `total`, `unit`, `ratePerSecond` and
`etaSeconds`; conversion measurements additionally include `percentage` (0–100)
and `etaScope: "phase"`. Unknown quantities are null. The terminal completion
event clears phase-local counts rather than relabelling them as whole-command
progress. Runtime errors retain `message` and
`causes`, exit nonzero and never emit a success result. Help and argument parsing
errors retain Clap's normal text output. Inspection output is unchanged.

The conversion result includes `mode`, `input`, `output`, `artifacts`,
`artifactStatus`, `diagnostics` and the qualified success `message`. In `--check`
mode artifacts are `planned`, not `published`. Diagnostics remain visible in both
the result and stderr; successful conversion does not establish render fidelity.

Read-only `inspect --metadata-only INPUT [--json]` lists the native target IDs and names, canvas
width/height, FPS, duration in seconds, and direct layer counts (AE) or direct
video/audio track counts (Premiere). Nested contents are not counted again.
Unavailable metadata is `null` in JSON and `unknown` in Premiere text output;
Premiere duration is the maximum readable direct clip end, without frame-rate
resampling, and stays unknown if an encountered range/reference cannot be read.
Metadata-only inspection neither probes media paths nor converts or publishes anything.
AE retains its richer composition/media inventory and existing JSON schema;
Premiere JSON uses `schema_version: 1`, `format`, and a `targets` array.
Default `inspect INPUT [--json]` (without `--metadata-only`) additionally inspects
the selected targets' source media and emits `schema_version: 2` JSON with
`media_admission`, `video_admission` and `media_preflight` for both Adobe formats;
it still never converts or publishes. Inspection rejects conversion flags.
The input format is inferred from `.aep`, `.prproj`, or `.tsrct` (case-insensitive);
for other extensions, specify `--from after-effects`, `--from premiere`, or
`--from tesseract`. Premiere ↔ Tesseract is supported in both directions. After
Effects export is limited to the experimental native subset below;
AEP-to-Premiere is unsupported. `--sequence <GUID>` applies only to
Premiere-to-Tesseract; `--composition <item-id>` only to AEP-to-Tesseract.
All supported directions write fresh directories, not individual files. Successful conversion commands print one short message on stdout by default (a result object with `convert --json`).
Both Premiere directions also print one stderr warning for each omitted feature or source unit and each approximated feature.
Premiere-to-Tesseract ends every warning except an approximation with ` (not converted)`.
Runtime errors print on stderr and exit nonzero. Premiere conversion requires
valid UTF-8 paths. A successful `--check` completes the
same conversion and validation without creating output.

All trait-based directions return typed diagnostics and the complete list of
planned (`Check`) or published (`Write`) project/media files, relative to the
output directory. The CLI retains its short stdout messages and stderr warnings
by default; `convert --json` exposes the report on stdout, without writing a
report or manifest sidecar file. Execution rechecks source and media identities
before publication.

## Automatic Premiere + AE packages

The normal `project.tsrct --to premiere -o output` command automatically uses
editable AEP picture scopes for source locations reported as unsupported or
approximated by the native converter. Native-supported picture and native audio
remain in Premiere. Native-only input does not produce an unnecessary AEP.
There is no manual layer selector or separate activation flag.

The package contains `project.prproj`, native `media/*`, and one or more
`media/ae-NNNN/compositions.aep` files with their AEP-local dependencies. The
Premiere links refer to these exact files and composition GUIDs. Open the package
with both Premiere and After Effects installed. Conversion itself does not launch
Adobe, prerender media or author a new geometry-script representation.

The normal `output/project.prproj --to tesseract -o imported` command resolves
linked compositions by file and GUID, then imports their editable contents at
each native occurrence's placement and clip clock, with the composition on its
own source clock and clipped to its canvas. It allocates separate IDs per
occurrence, packages footage under one namespace per resolved AEP and retains
native audio independently. Linked AEP audio is not supported and is diagnosed
rather than inferred. A missing link, an unsupported identity profile, a canvas
mismatch or a composition shorter than a clip's source range omits that clip with
a diagnostic; conflicting motion-blur settings are approximated.

Existing converter limitations still apply. A sequence of any canvas size
converts (custom canvas row below): an AEP scope's composition and its Premiere
link take the document canvas, and a linked composition imports only at the
frame that its link stream declares. CPU tests cover a 1080×1920 package both
ways (`hybrid_custom_canvas_scopes_and_links_keep_the_document_size_both_ways`)
and that frame check on a portrait sequence
(`a_custom_canvas_link_imports_only_at_the_frame_its_source_declares`); Adobe
open and render of a custom-canvas package are unmeasured. AEP scopes require
integral 24/25/30/50/60 fps and dependency-closed,
contiguous root scopes. On export, interleaved dependencies, unsafe nested-audio
replacement and incompatible source canvas/clocks fail explicitly.
AE feature omissions/approximations remain visible; an AEP fallback does not imply
that AE supports every FX feature. If AE omits an entire picture scope, the
existing native result is retained with both formats' diagnostics rather than
replaced by an empty AEP. If AE omits a layer that the native export kept, the
scope goes to whichever result loses fewer shown picture layers (`HYBRID-LINKED-PREFERRED`
or `HYBRID-NATIVE-RETAINED`, each with both counts: native omitted leaves plus
partly converted layers, against AE omitted leaves); an equal count goes to AE
only when it loses nothing the native export kept, otherwise the native,
Premiere-editable result stays. I/O and malformed-input failures still fail the command.

Check runs the same conversion and package validation without publishing. Write
requires a fresh destination; paths, file hashes, collisions and source drift
are checked, and recoverable failures remove only task-owned artifacts.
This is not crash-atomic or a filesystem lock.

Preparation retains all typed routing losses and source-layer owners; the former
1,024-event/layer/boundary and 1 MiB routing-text ceilings no longer abort larger
projects. Human-facing diagnostics retain their truncation limits, and packing
source tokens still reject integer exhaustion. Hierarchy, dependency, other packing
budgets and format limits remain. Native-only and hybrid output accept uncovered
timeline gaps without requiring an explicit black canvas or inserting one. Check
and Write use the same admission policy. Top-level gaps use Premiere's native
black background; nested gaps can remain transparent over lower content. This
is not a pixel/alpha-equivalence claim. Standalone audio is not a compositing
backdrop, though explicit audio dependencies remain subject to extraction checks.

**Adobe proof remains incomplete.** Actual CLI tests cover AEP generation,
packaged links, editable reimport, Check/Write and failure behavior. Ordinary
Premiere conversion imports each linked composition as an editable picture (see
the accuracy row below), with separate editable linked-audio placements that
export as native audio clips with diagnosed loss of grouping/linkage. Generated
package Premiere editing-UI acceptance, alpha/audio fidelity, live edits and
application relocation remain unverified. One large-project follow-up rendered
the generated `.prproj` through AME with linked AEPs; it is bounded execution
evidence, not independent visual equality. Historical generated Text output failed Adobe opening;
this activation does not repair or certify that feature. See the
[hybrid limitations](../../docs/hybrid-adobe-export.md) and
[AE feature support ledger](../../docs/after-effects-support.md).

## Timeline and media handling

Both import formats convert exactly one selected scene into `project.tsrct`.
Use `inspect INPUT` to find its native ID, then `--sequence <GUID>` for Premiere
or `--composition <item-id>` for AE. Selection may be omitted only when the source
contains exactly one selectable target; nested targets count too. Multiple
candidates reject before publication, even if only one is a root. Names may be
duplicated and are never selection keys. Selected nested dependencies still
convert; unrelated scenes do not. Explicit Premiere selection can also convert
the supported clips in a cyclic timeline, reporting unsupported occurrences.
This replaces Premiere's batch/ordinal filenames and AE's first-root fallback.

Tesseract-to-Premiere conversion creates `project.prproj` and `media/` from the current editable document,
including changed placements and deletions. Repeated placements share a media
asset while retaining independent source and timeline ranges. Media bytes,
extensions and content types are preserved. A layer with enabled Eye Contact
exports the active Eye Contact output that the renderer shows, not the original
upload; the original and the toggle are reported as not exported. An audio
layer exports its enhanced output while its enhancement is enabled, and
otherwise its original; the asset that does not play is never read, and it
and the toggle are reported as not exported. Non-ASCII or colliding media filenames
receive unique ASCII names. Conflicting live path aliases reject;
stale host-native paths are accepted only when the remaining aliases establish
media identity. A live absolute alias locates the media when every relative
hint is stale, for example after Adobe Save As outside the package. A live
package-local `RelativePath` identifies the media by itself, so other missing
relative hints are ignored as stale history; without one, a missing hint that
no live alias vouches for omits the media's placements. Media that
Premiere saved on Windows names drive-absolute paths (`C:\…`) that another host
cannot open. Such media resolves only through its package-local `RelativePath`,
read with `\` separators, and rejects without one. On a POSIX host, UNC,
device and drive-relative aliases still reject; a Windows host keeps its
unchanged native path handling.

Premiere publication hard-links staged files into the new output directory.
Where the output filesystem cannot link (for example FAT32, exFAT or an SMB
share), it copies each file into an exclusively created destination instead,
keeping the staged file's standard permissions (not ACLs or ownership);
neither path replaces an existing entry. The copy is tested only with injected
link refusals, not on a real FAT32, exFAT or SMB volume. Publication is not
atomic: a copied file is visible while it is written, and a process crash can
leave incomplete output; there is no completion marker. Treat only a
successful command exit as a completed conversion.

## Supported subset

After Effects import selects one composition and best-effort converts editable
layers, properties, shape/text content and available media with explicit warnings.
Import does not generate geometry scripts, but complex source/paint links or
Path motion may be copied statically or omitted with diagnostics. The complete
bidirectional workflow has not been established.

Experimental AEP export reads current edited FX values rather than replaying the
original native project. Consult the [AE format table](../../docs/formats/after-effects.md)
and [support ledger](../../docs/after-effects-support.md) for the current
feature-by-direction mappings, approximations, omissions and proof blockers.
The [test checkpoint](../../docs/after-effects-test-results.md) separates historical
editable assertions, Adobe acceptance/readback and measured render differences;
none establishes complete editable restoration or general fidelity. Relocating
the tooling does not rerun or upgrade that evidence.

AE conversions require an existing output parent and a nonexistent final directory
(including no dangling symlink). `--check` performs validation without creating final output, but may use temporary
staging (including export media preparation) and cannot guarantee future
filesystem writes will succeed.
Write mode stages and reopens the complete archive, then reserves the output
directory and publishes `project.tsrct` without overwriting. Export preflights and
encodes in both modes, then stages and publishes `project.aep` only in Write mode. This is not
crash-atomic directory publication: interruption can leave staging or an empty
output directory. Ordinary failures clean up only owned temporary/empty outputs;
concurrent foreign files are never recursively removed.

The Premiere subset is:

- Sequences of any canvas size, 1920×1080 or another positive size at the origin
  (portrait, square, ultrawide or odd), in square pixels; see the custom canvas
  row of the ledger for what another size keeps. Square-pixel, progressive 8-bit
  4:2:0 video (HEVC also 10-bit); HDR
  and other non-BT.709 colour passes through unchanged with one warning per file.
- Import sequences and media at 23.976, 24, 25, 29.97, 30, 50, 59.94, or 60 fps.
  Import uses nearest-millisecond boundaries and source-in. Export uses a 30 fps
  sequence, or the `--fps` rate, and preserves each media frame rate. Shared cuts are retained;
  identical source-frame selection is not guaranteed. A bounded final-frame hold
  covers rounding tails without shortening the clip. See the
  [timing rules](../../crates/premiere_file/README.md#timing) for the exact allowance.
- H.264 (`avc1`) or HEVC Main or Main 10 (`hvc1`, with or without an iPhone Dolby
  Vision record) in MP4 or QuickTime MOV, with matching source dimensions, at most one AAC audio stream and any number of ignored data tracks. Audio-only WAV, MP3, and
  M4A (AAC). Apple ProRes passes through to Premiere and linked AEP packages and is
  a transcode candidate on import. See [supported video formats](#supported-video-formats)
  for every video rule and rejection.
  A full-duration edit list may round up to the movie clock's precision (less than
  one movie tick and one source frame) or end less than one source frame early, as
  iPhone captures do; every sample is kept. Longer trims, shifted presentation
  starts, and speed changes remain unsupported.
- Still images (`IsStill` media): JPEG and single-frame PNG that the
  renderer's `image` decoder reads, of any size and without Exif rotation,
  including PNG straight alpha, as editable `ImageLayer`s at their pixel size
  with the clip's Motion, Opacity and their keys, mapped as on video in both
  directions, a static Crop as the image's Crop guide, and mapped effects as a
  flat video clip's after it; Linear Wipe, Track
  Matte Key and Scale to Frame Size fail closed. A still's
  Premiere source range, the clock of its keys, is synthetic (one hour into a
  twelve-hour clock, rounded down to a frame of the sequence rate) and is
  rebuilt from the layer's active range on export.
- Static, canvas-sized Color Matte and ordinary Black Video clips as editable
  solid-fill rectangles, with their static Opacity; ordinary Black Video requires
  an explicit stream `FrameRate` and exports as Color Matte, losing its generator
  identity. Neutral Color Mattes also retain one bounded sharp static Crop
  (zero feather, no retained effects or other mask/key combination).
  Default Cross Dissolve heads/tails on an eligible matte become editable linear
  Opacity ramps; canonical current ramps also export (see the ledger).
- Full-frame adjustment layers (a flagged clip of Black Video generator media) as FX
  adjustment layers over the layers below them, with their Opacity, Opacity keys and
  mapped effects. Static Position/uniform positive Scale with no active effect or
  static full RGB Invert changes the affected rectangle through an editable mask
  guide; Rotation, nondefault Anchor, Motion keys, Crop, wipes and retiming fail closed.
- Mono and stereo sound with static levels, mutes and Clip Gain, and the clip
  Volume Level (both project layouts) and matching stereo Amplify ducking curves
  as editable volume keys. Amplify's linear-dB ramps use diagnosed short-segment
  approximations; export writes edited keys as ordinary clip Volume, not ducking
  regeneration settings.
  Each sound placement is a separate editable audio layer; imported picture
  layers are silent. Export gives each sound placement its own audio track and
  writes its level and keys in the native clip Volume, with Clip Gain for a
  boost. Constant-rate/reverse physical sound imports as editable source-clock
  Audio playback and exports its currently edited speed/direction. Independent
  endpoints round to milliseconds; retimed gain/fades export as source-clock
  Level keys. Canonical native pitch ON/OFF import and edited constant-rate
  export use the independently saved two-field ON pair or OFF omission. No
  per-rate whitelist or imported-origin replay is used; unit acceptance and
  broader algorithm fidelity remain explicitly unverified. Retimed nested audio
  items and variable audio TimeRemapping remain unsupported; embedded sound of
  retimed video still reports an omission. Export also reports and drops source sound it cannot
  write, such as more than two channels or non-AAC MP4 audio, while the picture
  still exports. Audio fades and crossfades (Constant Gain, Constant Power,
  Exponential Fade) import as eased volume keys of the faded layers; a
  crossfade becomes two overlapping fades over the clips' source handles.
  Export writes each fade that keeps its imported key run at a layer edge as a
  one-sided audio transition. See the [audio rules](../../crates/premiere_file/README.md#audio).
- Multiple video tracks; placements must not overlap on the same track. The
  former 256-placement, 1024-expanded-layer and nested-read byte quotas are
  removed. Timing, depth, cycle and native field-width checks remain; see the
  the remaining bounds described below.
- Cuts, independent source trims, gaps, repeated media, and add/move/delete edits.
- Constant video speed and reverse in both directions, and Time Remapping ramps
  as editable FX playback keys on import. Export writes an exact FX frame hold
  as a native explicit Frame Hold, constant speed/reverse as native playback,
  and plateau-constrained forward nonlinear ramps as editable native Speed keys.
  Other bounded curves use diagnosed editable speed/Frame Hold segments, including
  holds and direction changes. Cuts snap to sequence frames; nonlinear legs use
  endpoint lines, not faithful easing. Reverse export approximates source-frame boundaries
  and can add endpoint Frame Holds. Native rendering can still select an adjacent source
  frame at interior sequence frames; export reports that limit. Passing image-similarity
  scores do not establish source-frame agreement.
  Segmented playback retains static appearance but omits animated controls and embedded
  sound with diagnostics. A clip whose native rate is not 1 loses its Motion and
  Opacity keys as reported features; a Linear Wipe on it omits the clip on
  import and stops the export. See the
  [retiming rules](../../crates/premiere_file/README.md#retiming).
- Placements of master clips with Adobe-saved Source Monitor In/Out marks. Each
  placement keeps its own absolute source In/Out, which may extend past the
  master range into the media. Export writes plain master-clip placements.
  Subclip placements are read the same way, as a further master clip on the
  same media, but that subclip shape is inferred, NOT Adobe-verified; no
  subclip is written.
- Clip Enable (native `ClipTrackItem/IsMuted`) as layer `isHidden` in both
  directions; a video track whose output (the "eye", `Track/IsMuted`) is off
  imports every clip on it hidden. Both field readings are AME-rendered on a
  derived fixture, not a Premiere-saved project (see the ledger). Disabled clips stay in the document with their source, trims and
  transform, and spans they alone cover are gaps.
- Explicit selection among independent or nested timelines and existing `.tsrct` save/reopen.
- Nested sequences on the outer canvas, at normal speed and the outer frame rate or
  at one constant forward speed with any inner rate: each placement becomes an
  editable group of independent, clipped copies that share media, hidden when
  the placement is, and moved by its Motion, static or keyed, without a Track
  Matte Key; a retimed one plays its inner window through a linear group
  playback. A plain group exports as a nested sequence whose placement
  carries the group's Motion, Opacity, keys, Crop or wipe, effects and Enable;
  a retimed group does not export.
  See the
  [nesting rules](../../crates/premiere_file/README.md#nested-sequences).
- Clip effect stacks in both directions: stack order and bypass (Premiere
  `Bypass` ↔ FX `EffectRecord` `enabled`) are kept and editable. Premiere
  applies a clip's effect chain in descending component `Index` (Index 0 last),
  which import and export map to FX's list order (Adobe evidence: Oracle run
  C6). One mapped
  effect is **Gaussian Blur**: Legacy `AE.ADBE Gaussian Blur 2` imports
  Blurriness unchanged, and the current (Film Impact) `AE.Impact_Blur_FX`
  imports its uniform subset as Blurriness = Amount × 5.7, the Legacy
  Blurriness of the same measured edge spread. Linear, Hold and Bezier keys use
  the shared Motion key path. Export writes the current Gaussian Blur with
  Amount = Blurriness / 5.7; a value above Blurriness 5700 (Amount 1000) omits
  the effect. The kernels, colour and edge rendering differ, and resolution
  scaling is unmeasured.
  Another is **Corner Pin** (`AE.ADBE Corner Pin`) ↔ FX
  `cornerPin`, with static or keyed corners that move on straight paths. The
  third is **Directional Blur**: Legacy `AE.ADBE Motion Blur` ↔ FX
  `directionalBlur`, with static or keyed Direction and Blur Length mapped
  through the clip's static uniform Scale and Rotation, and the current
  (Film Impact) `AE.Impact_Directional_Blur_FX` with Direction = Angle and
  Blur Length = Amount × 1.6. Export writes the current one. The fourth is
  **Levels** (`PR.ADBE Levels`) ↔ FX `levels`, with a static or keyed master
  row. The fifth is **Brightness & Contrast**
  (`AE.ADBE Brightness & Contrast 2`) ↔ FX `brightnessContrast`, with static or
  keyed Brightness and Contrast mapped unchanged; FX renders it with another
  model (see the ledger). The sixth is **Invert** (`AE.ADBE Invert`, Channel
  RGB) ↔ a FX `levels` with complementary outputs, with a static or keyed
  Blend With Original. The seventh and eighth are **Tint** (`AE.ADBE Tint`)
  and Premiere 26.5.1's **Black & White** (`AE.ADBE Black & White`) ↔ FX
  `tintTritone`, with static or keyed (Linear, Hold) Map Black To and Map
  White To colours and a static or keyed Amount to Tint; a Black & White is
  Tint's defaults and exports as a Tint. The ninth is **Ramp** (`AE.ADBE
  Ramp`) ↔ FX `gradientRamp`, for a linear, axis-aligned ramp without scatter
  on a clip whose frame is the canvas, with static or keyed endpoints,
  colours and Blend With Original. The tenth is **Mosaic** (`AE.ADBE Mosaic`,
  "Mosaic (Legacy)") ↔ FX `mosaic`, for Sharp Colors on with whole block
  counts, static or with Hold keys, on a media clip at any Motion. The
  eleventh is **Replicate** (`AE.ADBE Replicate`) ↔ FX `motionTile`, as
  Count × Count whole copies for a whole Count from 2 to 16, static or with
  Hold keys, on a clip whose frame is the canvas; both directions report it
  as an approximation. The twelfth is **Posterize** (`AE.ADBE Posterize`) ↔
  FX `posterize`, for whole Levels, static or with Hold keys, converted with one approximation
  warning each: Premiere's equal-bin quantizer is not the FX shader's
  nearest level. The thirteenth is **Transform** (`AE.ADBE Geometry`):
  the one Transform of a
  sequence-sized media clip without a Crop, Linear Wipe or Opacity mask
  becomes the transform of the clip's video under a mask-less stage group
  that carries the Motion, with Position, Scale and Rotation keys as the
  video's tracks, and such a stage exports back as one clip whose chain ends
  in a Transform; its Opacity, motion blur, bicubic Sampling and unmeasured
  skews convert approximately, with one warning each.
  **Find Edges** (`AE.ADBE Find Edges`) ↔ FX `findEdges` retains static
  Invert with opposite numeric polarity (Adobe checked → FX 0; unchecked →
  FX 1), on an unstaged full-canvas host at identity static Motion. Blend With
  Original and its keys have no FX equivalent and are diagnosed as omitted;
  export writes blend 0 from current FX values. The grayscale Sobel detector
  is an approximation; native render/readback and alpha fidelity are unmeasured.
  `AE.ADBE Geometry2` on media retains the centered/off-centre positive
  uniform zoom mapping as editable Corner Pin (off-centre raster fidelity is
  unmeasured); the existing scale-only adjustment zoom keeps that mapping.
  Outside that subset, a neutral adjustment with Geometry2 alone imports as
  an editable composed Group G × M with a nonpainting sequence-canvas Rect/Add
  guide. Position, positive uniform Scale, Rotation and Anchor tracks retain
  easing and signed/out-of-window keys; lower intrinsic Motion and media clocks
  remain independent, and upper siblings stay outside. Complete coextensive
  lower occurrences are required; crossing windows and competing adjustment
  effects are diagnosed without moving or dropping the lower picture. Intrinsic
  adjustment Motion remains the separate coverage mapping, not Geometry2.
  Export reads the current Group/guide and writes an identity nest containing
  lower children and a true adjustment with native Geometry2, not Corner Pin or
  a baked picture. Authored Group Opacity instead exports through an explicitly
  diagnosed ordinary moved nest with native Motion/Opacity keys, retaining the
  lower picture and current affine controls after guide validation. It does not
  map the native adjustment's unmeasured opacity mix; fallback sampling/clipping
  fidelity is unmeasured. Unsupported affine edits on the native Geometry2 path
  (such as non-uniform Scale/Scale Width keys or Skew) can omit the whole stage,
  lower picture included. A trimmed Opacity-fallback Group that outlasts its
  exported lower picture can likewise be omitted by existing nest-tail admission.
  These omissions are diagnosed; the fallback is not general Motion admission.
  Edited Rotation and animated Anchor have structural wire and
  reimport coverage; independent Adobe reopening of exported projects is unrun.
  Constant-rate inner media clips retain proportional source trims and editable
  playback inside nested windows. Explicit physical-video Frame Hold children keep
  their held source through unit-forward, matching-rate window trims and export
  current hold edits inside ordinary nests. Held children with own-effect keys on an
  unsupported held clock are omitted contextually; variable inner remapping remains omitted.
  Native-derived regressions `native_opening_slow_clip_trims_to_the_nested_window`
  and `native_geometry2_adjustment_zoom_keeps_editable_corner_keys` bind the
  selected source fields ([provenance](../../crates/premiere_file/tests/fixtures/cap2-native-import.md)).
  An unsupported active keying, Radial Wipe or masked effect omits its whole
  clip. One supported effect mask uses an isolated editable adjustment scope
  with the reported filter-edge approximation described below. A blur or Corner Pin converts on either side of an active Crop or
  supported cardinal Linear Wipe: one that applies after the mask (a lower chain
  `Index`) stays on one video layer, and one that applies before it (a higher
  `Index`) goes under a group that carries the clip's Motion, Opacity and mask
  and exports back as one clip; converted effects on both sides are omitted.
  Other unmapped effects,
  active or bypassed, are omitted and reported with
  its match name, display name, record versions, stack position (1 is the first
  effect applied), clip, track and time range; its clip and the other effects
  stay. See the ledger below.
- Single-style graphic text: a Premiere 26 Type-tool graphic with one text layer
  converts to an editable FX text layer, and a root FX text layer exports as
  one. Wording, font, size, fill, outside stroke, all caps, tracking, leading,
  left/center/right/justified alignment, point text, box text with top/center/
  bottom alignment, and a static position, anchor, uniform scale, rotation and
  opacity convert. Position, uniform Scale, Rotation and Opacity keys of the
  text with Linear or Hold timing convert in both directions as editable FX
  keys, including keys outside the trimmed graphic (JRB-1990). Bezier keys
  convert on Text Scale and Opacity and Vector Motion Scale and Rotation,
  whose speed unit an Adobe probe measured; on Position and Text Rotation
  they wait for a probe (see the ledger).
  A static Vector Motion is composed into the text transform and its keys;
  keyed Vector Motion becomes an FX graphic group whose transform and keys
  move the text, and such a group exports as keyed Vector Motion. The
  graphic clip's own Opacity and its keys (Bezier included) become the
  opacity of such a group, and back (JRB-1990), and its Blend Mode the
  group's (Opacity Blend Mode row in the ledger).
  Text-only
  timelines convert. Box-text import is verified only with
  generated payloads that Premiere 26.5.1 rendered; no Premiere-authored box
  text is pinned. A box-text payload with other fields is omitted, not
  approximated.
- Static text shadow: an enabled Premiere text shadow on filled, unscaled,
  unrotated text becomes one editable FX `DropShadow` effect on the text layer,
  drawn under its fill and stroke, and one static `DropShadow` on such a text
  layer exports as the Premiere shadow. The angle, distance, blur, size and
  opacity mapping is Adobe-verified by AME calibration renders; see the
  ledger. The stroke stays the text's own outside stroke above.
- Graphic objects and static vector shapes (JRB-1989): a Premiere 26 graphic
  with several Text and Shape objects becomes an FX group whose layers are
  its objects, the first listed in front, with the Vector Motion as the group
  transform; one Shape becomes an FX shape layer. A Shape's path, its solid
  fill or none, a centred stroke with butt caps at corners whose join Adobe
  renders measured, its static transform, including a nonuniform scale, and
  a hard shadow convert in both directions, and such groups and root shape
  layers export as graphics. See the ledger.
- Caption tracks: each Premiere caption cue becomes an editable FX text layer
  timed to the cue and painted above the video, a higher caption track above a
  lower one. Cues on different caption tracks stay separate layers, so
  overlaps and gaps are kept; layers are named like `C1 caption 2` (track,
  then cue position). A cue converts when it has single-style wording (line
  breaks included), PostScript font, size, fill, stroke, shadow and
  background box at Premiere's bottom default, read from the cue's own
  payload (Adobe-verified: Premiere draws a cue's own style, its track's
  template is only a default); the stroke and shadow map as for Type-tool
  text, and a 48 px cue's background box becomes an FX group box around the
  text (see the ledger). It becomes center- or left-justified, bottom-aligned
  box text whose last line ends where Premiere draws it: the baseline at 95%
  of the frame height (y 1026 on 1080p), centred, with the pivot on that
  baseline and earlier lines stacked above it (Adobe-verified; see the
  ledger); a left cue starts 12.5% of the frame width in (inferred). A higher
  caption track paints above a lower one (Adobe-verified). Cues on a hidden
  caption track and disabled cues import as hidden layers (inferred). Exported
  caption layers are Type-tool graphics, not caption tracks; a hidden caption
  layer exports disabled (clip Enable off), as other hidden text does.

Imported text keeps Premiere's PostScript font name; an empty Text saved
without a font imports in Inter Regular. Conversion embeds no font
files, so import reports each font once; add it with `tsrct project import-font`
before preview or export. Export needs a stored PostScript name or a packaged
font; otherwise it omits the text. See the [font rules](../../crates/premiere_file/README.md#fonts).

Text graphics with mixed styles, keys on a
parameter without a mapping (anchors, Horizontal Scale, Scale Width, parent and
selection parameters), Bezier keys on Position or Text Rotation (until their
speed unit is verified), a Bezier key right after a Linear or Hold key whose
in-handle, or the Linear key's stored out-handle, is not exactly neutral (until an Adobe probe covers it), small caps, other full-justify modes, outline-only strokes,
nonuniform text or graphic scale, line spacing tighter than 0.8 em,
responsive-design or mask settings, nondefault clip-level Motion, or
pre-26 Source Text encodings are omitted as occurrences. An
enabled Type-tool text background (calibrated for caption cues only), a shadow on scaled, rotated or unfilled
text, on text with Scale or Rotation keys or under a Vector Motion that scales
or rotates, and a shadow with an out-of-range value (opacity outside 0–100, a
non-finite angle, or a negative or non-finite distance, size or blur) are
omitted as features and the text stays. Source Text keys convert as Hold
keys of the text and of the size, fill, tracking, leading, stroke switch and
all caps (JRB-1990). Held stroke widths with one explicit, constant stroke
color use an editable all-character text animator: the static FX width is
2× the native width and the keys add deltas to that base (JRB-1992).
Export reconstructs widths from the current authored base and delta keys;
only one selector-free, width-only animator with independent enabled Hold
keys is supported. Other text animators remain unsupported. Keys that change
the font, justification, box, shadow or enabled stroke color omit the graphic.
An absent run marker (Source Text style slot 24) is diagnosed without dropping
known styling; explicit unknown marker values still reject. An enabled stroke
without an explicit color remains unsupported. Native reopen/render fidelity
of the keyed-width mapping is unverified.
Export omits, with a diagnostic, a text layer that has masks or a track matte,
unsupported animators, path or anchor options, font variations, underline, strikethrough,
baseline shift, an authored box first baseline, stroke over fill, nonuniform
scale, translucent paints, line spacing below 0.8 em, or a family/style that
the document does not package. Hidden text exports disabled (clip Enable off).
Description, parenting, the blend mode of a text layer inside a graphic group
(an ungrouped text's exports on its graphic, and a graphic group's with its
clip Opacity), motion blur, skew, 3D
rotation and animation other than Position, uniform Scale, Rotation, Opacity
and the Source Text keys of a text whose content is keyed are omitted as
features and the static text exports, as for video layers; a text layer whose
Source Text tracks Premiere cannot hold (an easing other than Hold after the
first key, or a document the encoding cannot write) is omitted as an
occurrence, because static text would show the wrong content; keys that
Premiere cannot hold, and keys with cubic Bezier easing on
Position or Text Rotation until their speed unit is verified, are omitted the
same way. Each effect except one static, enabled, normal-blend drop
shadow is omitted as a feature and named: Premiere text has no inner shadow,
satin, bevel, glow or gradient overlay, and no field of a stroke effect is
readable outside `fx_schema`. Several, disabled or animated (JRB-1990)
drop shadows, a shadow on scaled, rotated or unfilled text, on text with Scale
or Rotation keys or in a group that scales or rotates, and a shadow whose
color, offset or values do not fit Premiere's ranges are omitted the same way.
A group of text and shape layers exports as one graphic, under the same text
rules; a group property that a graphic cannot carry omits it (see the
ledger).
Legacy Titles, captions and MOGRTs are not text graphics.

A caption cue with a disabled fill, all caps, tracking or leading, right or
full justification, another alignment, a text box, a stored position
(document slot 33), several text blocks, effects, retiming or a playback speed
is omitted as an occurrence whose reason names the attribute, for example
`C1 caption 12: unsupported conversion: all caps not converted`. A cue's
background is omitted as a feature, the text kept, when its text size is not
48 px, its opacity is not 100, its corner radius exceeds half the box's least
height, or its payload omits a background slot (`C1 caption 12: text
background not converted: …`). A cue that overlaps an earlier cue on its track, a
repeated or dangling cue reference and a malformed cue `IsMuted` omit that cue.
A caption track is omitted whole when it repeats another track's Index, has a
malformed `IsMuted`, lacks a decodable template style, or holds more cues than
remain of the sequence's 256 timed items after its video and audio clips and
lower caption tracks. Caption content never makes a timeline fail.

Unsupported content in selected video structures is reported rather than silently
dropped. Unused project-panel records and descriptive media metadata are not
converted or reported. Opacity, most transforms/animation,
video transitions, retiming (including reverse or remapped nests, retimed-nest Motion keys and interpolation, and sound), other text and effects other than
Gaussian Blur, Corner Pin, Directional Blur, Levels, Brightness & Contrast, Invert, Find Edges, Tint, Black & White, Ramp, Mosaic, Replicate, Posterize and Transform are not supported. Track, master and source audio automation,
keyed Mute, pan, other insert effects, and audio transitions outside the audio
transition rows are reported and not converted. TIFF/PSD/GIF/WebP/animated PNG, 12-bit or
lossless JPEG, Exif orientation other than 1, other generator stills
(titles, graphics, Transparent Video), image sequences, `AlphaType`
other than absent or `1`, `IgnoreAlpha` on a transparent still, and a Linear
Wipe on a still omit the occurrence on import, as does Scale to Frame Size on
any placement. An occurrence that cannot convert is omitted; valid occurrences
remain. The measured default Film Impact Cross Dissolve one-sided profile imports
as two editable smoothstep Opacity keys on an unstaged still, video or linked
composition's outer clip group on the document clock. Two-sided fades,
custom controls and masked, staged, retimed or already opacity-keyed hosts remain
omitted; the temporal curve and encoded-value compositing are approximate.
Default `AE.ADBE Cross Dissolve New` heads/tails use editable linear picture
opacity on eligible physical or linked pictures. Two-sided static, flat Normal
pictures with unit-speed source handles use isolated weighted groups. Canonical
current controls export native transitions and clip links; noncanonical edits
use ordinary group export with scoped diagnostics. See the bounded profile below.
Other effects, markers, video transitions, and data tracks are omitted from
otherwise convertible content. On video and stills, static Motion (Position, Anchor Point,
Scale per axis, Rotation) and Opacity convert, and so do intrinsic Motion
Position, Rotation and **uniform Scale** keys and Opacity keys, in both
directions. Uniform Scale becomes a pair of independently editable FX
ScaleX/ScaleY tracks; export requires identical values, times, and easing on
both axes and otherwise omits the Scale keys as a feature. Position becomes
paired FX PositionX/PositionY tracks in canvas pixels. Premiere interpolation
code `0` imports as Linear, `4` as Hold, and `5` (Bezier) as an FX cubic Bezier
whose handles come from the native speed and influence; export writes FX
Linear, Hold and cubic keys back as native Linear, Hold and Bezier speed and
influence. Another numeric mode, or a Bezier that the FX curve cannot represent,
omits the occurrence on import. Edited values, times, easing and Hold steps are
exported rather than the original XML. Native tick times round to signed
milliseconds on import (up to 0.5 ms error); exported times are multiples of one
millisecond. Two distinct keys that round to the same millisecond omit that
property's keys as a feature rather than silently merging; the clip and its
other keys still convert. Premiere holds a keyed property's first key before
that key, also when the source In comes earlier, so its static value
(`StartKeyframe`) never shows; import keeps the keys and the FX animator holds
the first key the same way. Export writes a first key after the source In
unchanged, whatever the FX static value. The secondary interpolation field, per-key native
metadata and the last key's unused outgoing interpolation are lost. Motion key
data with an explicit `IsTimeVarying=false` flag rejects instead of activating
disabled animation; unknown `IsTimeVarying` values also reject. Another animated
Motion parameter, such as Scale Width or Anchor Point, still rejects. Motion and
Opacity keys on a clip whose native rate is not 1 are omitted as features (see
the retiming row).
Apart from the export gates in the ledger below, the generated Motion graph has
**not** had a Premiere 26.x reopen/render validation.

Unlisted frame rates, variable-rate media, source rotation, full-range interpretations,
non-square pixels, frames off the origin, empty timelines and fragmented MP4 may omit an occurrence or
an entire timeline. Malformed input, publication errors and a project with no
convertible timeline reject. Document duration follows the last video track item
(see the duration rows below). Media sample counts are capped at 10 million.
A MOV extension does not imply support for every QuickTime codec. Native records
are grounded in Premiere 26.x projects, with Adobe validation on 26.5.1; earlier
versions are not yet validated and may reject on different serialization.
Caption records are the exception: they come from one Premiere 25.5 project,
and no Premiere 26 caption sample exists.

Generated Tesseract documents include an editable black rectangle behind the video
because the current renderer does not render uncovered gaps as black from
`backgroundColor` alone. Export still recognizes an unmodified bottommost black
rectangle as that canvas, but does not require one or require its range to cover
gaps. It does not insert a background or change timeline content to conceal gaps.
The document background may be absent or black.

Authored **Color Matte** clips are not that canvas: each placement imports as
its own full-frame rectangle (`Premiere color matte N`) at its static Opacity (which a
canonical Cross Dissolve head or tail can key), and time covered
only by a matte is content, not a gap. A neutral matte's sharp static Crop binds
an Add mask to a nonpainting sibling rectangle, without packaging matte footage.
The owner keeps its color, range, enable state and static Opacity; the guide
shares its parent/range and remains opaque and nonpainting for a disabled owner.
Feathered, keyed or Zoom Crop, retained standard/source effects, source Transform
staging, nonneutral Motion, Motion/Opacity keys and mixed masks remain unsupported.
Source-derived tests verify editable structure, not cropped-matte visual/alpha
fidelity, full-project recovery or cropped-matte export qualification.
Export turns every static full-frame
solid rectangle into a Color Matte item, whether or not it was imported, except
a rectangle that a mask references, which is a guide that FX never paints, and a
bottommost black one with the origin pivot, which is read as the canvas. Any
other rectangle exports as a graphic Shape (see the ledger); the Shape rules
omit a keyed one, such as a faded matte.

Successful conversion verifies structure and packaging, not pixel/audio fidelity.
Rendering uses the existing engine; known picture, source-frame sampling and audio
length differences remain separate limitations. Generated native outputs require
independent Premiere validation; converting them back to Tesseract is not a
substitute.

## Conversion accuracy and limitations

### Modern/Legacy Noise and Grain

Premiere → FX retains both native Noise components in reverse native Index order
(Modern550 before Legacy549) and retains their owner and bypass. Legacy Amount
and Modern Intensity use `Grain.amount = native strength × 0.4`: Legacy5→2,
Modern50→20. This monotonic normalization is not calibration. Modern Seed8041
maps to editable seed; Intensity3 and Seed8041 support existing Linear/Hold scalar
keys and clock guards. Saved tonal controls (75 each), Uniform Intensity, Saturation50,
Blend Mode4 and Master100 are validated but not reproduced. Nondefault static
values have the same diagnosed losses. No native flavor bytes are replayed.

Grain adds independent RGB noise in straight-alpha space, clamps RGB and retains
alpha. Its amplitude is ±0.025 × amount. Native tonal weighting, saturation,
compositing, RNG distribution, seed sequence, spatial and temporal behavior differ;
Preserve Alpha false approximates alpha preservation. Legacy monochrome/wrapping
modes now retain color/clipped Grain instead of dropping the effect. Animated
unmapped native controls remain unsupported. RGB/alpha error is unmeasured.

FX → Premiere writes CURRENT Grain amount0–40 as canonical Legacy Amount0–100,
including bypass and Linear/Hold strength keys. Keyed Legacy Amount explicitly sets
`IsTimeVarying=true`; scalar key tuples alone are dormant in Premiere 26.5.2.
Controlled native activation established this flag requirement without changing
keys or values; fresh corrected-export native validation remains separate.
Static controls remain static. `intensity` is an accepted alias;
conflicting amount/intensity tracks remain rejected. Static size/softness/aspect/seed
edits (including default softness1) now retain strength with explicit lost-control
diagnostics instead of dropping Grain. Their animation remains unsupported. Invalid
strength and unsafe clock restrictions remain. Successful native Grain uses the
producer-specific retained-native loss route, not global diagnostic suppression;
actual failed effects still trigger normal hybrid fallback.

The unchanged [native record fixture](../../crates/premiere_file/tests/fixtures/noise-native-records.md)
pins controls/order. Source-derived tests cover both effects and exact-record
strength/seed/malformed edits; supplementary public CLI tests inspect final native
components, edited values, order/bypass and actual unsupported fallback. Structural
tests do not prove Adobe acceptance: native readback/render/RGB/alpha proof for
Modern Noise is unrun/unmeasured, not a pass.

Case names below identify recorded observations, not bundled run-report paths or
current-checkout verification. Supplementary screenshots and execution journals
are not included. See the [corpus contract](../../tests/README.md) for pinned
references, current case selection and the distinction between structural and
visual proof.

**Agents changing Premiere ↔ Tesseract conversion MUST update this ledger in the
same PR whenever they add, remove, or change a supported mapping, approximation,
precision limit, or rejection.** Record the direction, affected property/mode,
exact information lost, numerical/visual error bound (or say *unbounded*), and
whether the evidence is a synthetic test or a Premiere application test. Add or
update a regression test for the affected case. A successful self-roundtrip is
**not** Premiere reopen/render proof. If a new conversion is exact, explicitly
record that it adds no new loss rather than leaving this ledger unchanged. Review
this section before approving conversion changes; do not silently flatten keys
or hide unsupported animated properties. This is an **internal** engineering
inventory, not a CLI warning or a public conversion-report API. #4306's omitted
source-content warnings are a different contract and must not be conflated with
retained-but-approximated keys.

Visibility is orthogonal to playback and transition fields.

| Direction / case | What changes or is lost | Bound and evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Premiere → FX: Find Edges (`AE.ADBE Find Edges`, JRB-2128) | Static native Invert maps to editable `findEdges.invert`: checked → 0 (bright edges on black), unchecked → 1 (dark edges on white), opposite to the shader's numeric polarity. On matching full-canvas, unstaged identity-static-Motion hosts, the existing grayscale Sobel detector approximates Adobe's colored edge detector. Nonzero or animated Blend With Original is omitted with a contextual diagnostic; full-strength edges remain. Animated Invert, malformed controls and other sampling frames omit only the effect, not the clip. No JS, baked media or hidden source replay. | **Unbounded visual error; structural only.** The [native control fixture](../../crates/premiere_file/tests/fixtures/find-edges-26.5.xml) retains source identity, the true checkbox and Linear blend keys. Adobe's documented polarity and the actual FX shader establish the reversal, not names. `find_edges_native_controls_import_editably_with_explicit_losses` uses those records in a supported CPU clip harness; `find_edges_unsupported_controls_keep_the_clip` covers unsupported animation and sampling frames. Whole human-sequence/media import, native output, RGB/alpha comparison and other hosts are unmeasured. |
| FX → Premiere: edited Find Edges (JRB-2128) | Current FX invert is written as native `invert <= 0.5`; absent uses FX's default 1 (native unchecked). Blend With Original is 0, with no blend keys or native replay. Native 9/7 component and version-10 checkbox/numeric records retain editability. Disabled emits Bypass true (inferred, not in the native witness). Effect-property animation and staged/nested or nonmatching/animated-Motion frames omit only the effect with diagnostics. The native detector differs from FX's grayscale Sobel, reported as an approximation. | **Unbounded visual error; structural only.** `find_edges_edited_export_writes_current_polarity_and_native_layout` imports the native controls, edits current values and enabled state, then checks generated XML independently of our reader, including threshold/default polarity, class/parameter IDs, omitted checkbox Name, bounds and absent keys. Unsupported-property and frame tests are supplemental synthetic evidence. No Adobe reopen/readback or render, no visual/alpha pass. |
| Premiere → FX: default Film Impact one-sided Cross Dissolve (`AE.AE_Impact_Dissolve`) | One-sided heads or tails with the measured 30-control static profile (Start/End 0/100, Ease 34/34, Types 0, no focus/custom curve, Applied Version 260300) become two editable Opacity keys on their resolved retained occurrence: current opacity at fade start to zero at clip end for tails; zero at clip start to current opacity at fade end for heads, with incoming cubic `(1/3,0,2/3,1)`, a chosen smoothstep approximation. The host must have Normal blend and unit forward playback, no Opacity keys, Crop/wipe/mask/matte or Transform stage, and no competing transition; it is an unstaged physical still/video or the outer Premiere group of a linked composition on the document clock. Linked content, its clock seed, source group, children and AE diagnostics are unchanged. Other profiles and two-sided/nested fades omit only the transition. Native transition export remains rejected; an imported opacity track exports through the existing key writer, without recreating the plugin. | **Approximation, unbounded visual error; no strict visual pass.** Absolute transition boundaries round half-up to milliseconds before subtracting the rounded clip start (≤0.5 ms per absolute boundary); source clocks/ranges do not change. One Bonsa native tail's measured effective alpha in a linearized basis fits smoothstep with RMSE 0.00746/max 0.0150; a separate native head fits the mirrored smoothstep with RMSE 0.00504/max 0.00897. These compressed-shot measurements are not a general plugin equation or output error bound. Native linear-light blending differs from FX SDR encoded-value compositing, with content-dependent residual. Native-source-backed profile and editable-key regressions plus linked-group preservation tests establish structure. Whole-cadence focused independent render proof remains pending. |
| Premiere ↔ FX: default Cross Dissolve (`AE.ADBE Cross Dissolve New`, displayed as Legacy) | Eligible one-sided heads/tails use linear editable Opacity on retained physical pictures or linked-composition groups. Two-sided static, flat Normal video/still/Color Matte pictures require unit-speed source handles; two Add fades inside a masked isolation group preserve their independent opacity. Native clocks/cut weights precede millisecond rounding. Two-sided animation, effects, masks, linked/nested sources and multiple-transition pictures remain unsupported. A competing Film Impact opacity owner is retained rather than overwritten. Canonical current controls export native transition records/links; one-sided matte peaks include current fill alpha. Noncanonical trims/rates/blends use ordinary export, which may omit unsupported nested windows with scoped diagnostics, not restore trimmed handles or abort an independent supported picture. Whole-owner replacement removes its transition; unrelated replacements retain links. | **Bounded evidence; no general visual error bound.** Absolute boundaries round by at most 0.5 ms; native residual ticks are restored only when current rounded controls agree. Structural tests cover current edits, links, translucent-matte opacity, fallback and replacement ownership. One opaque four-second video-to-matte import control passed 17 native RGB samples at 0.25 s (mean 0.999955, minimum 0.999841; required 0.99/0.98). A current 1.6–2.4 s export rendered in Premiere and retained transition/picture clocks after save/reopen. Rendered alpha, other color spaces and arbitrary edited exports remain unverified. |
| Premiere → FX: measured default Film Impact Pop entrance | Static 43-control incoming 30/32-frame profile becomes editable scale/position keys on guarded flat pictures, preserving source clocks and independent tail Dissolve opacity. [Subset and limits](../../docs/formats/premiere.md#measured-default-film-impact-pop-geometry). | Sixteen measured geometry knots approximate the spring; early exposure/motion blur and general spring/fade laws remain unproved. Native comparisons are diagnostic; no general parity or native export reconstruction claim. |
| Premiere → FX: measured neutral Film Impact Stroke | Exact static white square profiles Size 6/Prescale 99 or 100 and Size 66/99 retain editable picture-owner geometry on guarded opaque videos. [Subset and limits](../../docs/formats/premiere.md#measured-neutral-film-impact-stroke-profiles). | Size 6 uses existing outside Stroke; Size 66/99 uses an explicitly approximate source-bounds backplate. General size law, alpha outlines and native rasterization remain unverified; import only. |

An AME render of a Premiere 26.5.1 project (Oracle run C, pinned as Asset `rHNCcNehstSSoGsiUyR2_vid`) shows (18, 0) as Normal: at 100% the red source colour over the blue clip, and a uniform blend at partial opacity; the case is not scored (linear-light gap). That project, `premiere_isolated_motion_opacity_26_5`, converts every clip with its static values and keys, keeps unique keyframe ids in both copies of its keyed nest, and reads back with the same values after an export (`premiere_26_5_motion_and_opacity_stay_editable_in_both_directions`, structural). Export gate (`export_gate` in the case's `comparison.json`): the export writes Normal as (18, 0). Adobe reopen: Premiere 26.5.1 opens it with no warning text and no offline media, and reads all 12 clips with (18, 0) and the values and keys it reads from the fixture, except S2's Scale Width (60 for 100, Uniform Scale on). Adobe render: AME renders the export with decoded frames identical to the fixture render (300 of 300, MSE 0). The first gate, whose export wrote (22, 10), stopped on that pair and is superseded. |
| Premiere → FX: static Motion Crop (Premiere 26.5 Motion layout) | Motion parameters 8–11 (Crop Left, Top, Right and Bottom: edge percentages of the clip's own media frame, cropped before Motion moves it; no feather or zoom) become the clip's Crop and import through the existing Crop mapping: a `RectLayer` guide in source pixels beside the video, sharing its transform and converted Motion keys, as one Add mask with zero feather. Premiere applies Motion, and so its Crop, after every standard effect, so the reader counts every converted effect as applied before it (as for an Opacity mask) and a clip with one stages (clip processing stage row); Motion Crop with a Linear Wipe, Opacity mask or Track Matte Key follows the Crop rules of those rows, and a Transform effect beside it is omitted as beside a Crop. The static value is the `StartKeyframe` value; a cached `CurrentValue` is ignored, as on every Motion parameter. **Omitted and reported** (occurrence): keyed or time-varying edges (`animated Motion Crop … is unsupported`: keys are unmeasured and the static value would crop wrongly between them), a nonfinite or unparsable value, an edge outside 0–100 or opposing edges without a visible area (`…: Motion Crop: invalid Premiere project: …`), and a Motion Crop beside an active non-default Crop effect (`a Motion Crop beside an active Crop effect on one clip is not converted`: each applies at its own stage and the clip keeps one mask). A still's static Motion Crop imports as the image's Crop guide, beside the image in its own pixel frame with its transform and Motion keys (a hidden still keeps its guide), and exports as the still clip's Crop effect (FX → Premiere still image row), which import reads back as the same guide (Premiere → FX still image row). A Color Matte, adjustment layer or nest with a Motion Crop is omitted by its existing edit rules (a nest: `Motion Crop on a nested sequence occurrence is not converted`), not drawn uncropped. Export: FX keeps one Crop form, so an imported Motion Crop exports as the existing `AE.ADBE AECrop` effect (26.3 layout, after the video's effects) with the 26.3 Motion, which has no Motion Crop: the native control changes, and the edges keep their values up to floating-point rounding. **Before**, every nonzero Motion Crop omitted its occurrence (`nonzero or keyed Motion Crop … is unsupported`), and stills and nests were omitted with that message. | Geometry is the Crop mapping's (edge percentages times the source frame, in the video's frame): no new loss for the mapped form. Evidence is **structural**: derived edits of Premiere 26.5.1 saves (`feature_motion_opacity_26_5_strict`, `feature_images_nests_26_5`, and `feature_stage_motion_26_5_strict`, whose 1080x1920 clip H on the 1920x1080 sequence takes its Crop effect's edges as a Motion Crop at an off-centre Position); no pinned Adobe fixture has a nonzero Motion Crop, so no Adobe render or reopen is pinned. **Inferred**: the order after effects (from Motion's measured order; no Motion Crop renders beside an effect), Rotation and keyed Motion (carried as for a Crop effect), and the export's Crop effect (not reopened in Premiere). Tests: `premiere_26_5_static_motion_crop_imports_as_a_crop_guide_in_the_clip_frame`, `premiere_26_5_keyed_or_invalid_motion_crop_omits_the_clip`, `motion_crop_applies_after_every_standard_effect`, `motion_crop_beside_a_crop_effect_or_linear_wipe_omits_the_occurrence`, `a_still_motion_crop_becomes_its_image_crop_guide`, `a_still_motion_crop_that_cannot_convert_still_omits_the_still` and the public `premiere_26_5_static_motion_crop_imports_as_a_crop_guide_and_exports_as_a_crop_effect` and `a_motion_crop_crops_its_portrait_media_frame_after_its_effects_and_round_trips` (import, export as a Crop effect, reimport; the second stages H above its blur where the saved Crop effect keeps it flat); they replace `premiere_26_5_nonzero_or_keyed_motion_crop_omits_the_clip`. |

| Either direction: Opacity Blend Mode (JRB-2081; conversion audit A1) | Import reads the mode from parameter 2 of the Opacity (both layouts): 26 codes convert to the FX mode of the same name (Linear Dodge (Add) → `add`) on every placement that carries an Opacity: a media clip (flat: its layer; staged: its stage group, whose video composites Normal), a still, a Color Matte, an adjustment layer (FX composites the effected copy over the untouched composite with the mode, as with its Opacity), a graphic (its group; a blended graphic of one object is grouped) and a nested sequence placement (its group). Premiere's Dissolve (6), code 27 and any other value are **approximated** as Normal with one warning (`Blend Mode (P, L) has no measured Premiere mode; converted as Normal, …`); no placement is omitted for its blend. Export writes each FX mode as the chart's canonical pair (`PrBlendMode::native_values`; that the Blend Mode menu writes the same parameter 3 is inferred) on every such host; FX's classic After Effects modes export as the nearest Premiere mode (Classic Color Burn → Color Burn and Classic Color Dodge → Color Dodge with one warning each, Classic Difference → Difference exactly), so no clip, stage group, nest, graphic, adjustment layer, Color Matte or still is omitted for its blend. An ungrouped text or shape layer, and a rectangle that exports as a Shape, write their blend on their graphic; a Premiere graphic's objects have no blend of their own, so the blend of an object inside a graphic group exports as Normal with one warning (`unsupported blend mode was not exported (using normal)`) and the graphic keeps its other objects. A video that exports as the track matte source of another clip writes Normal with the same warning: how a Track Matte Key reads a blended matte clip is unmeasured. A nest whose group neither blends nor is matted reports once when a layer inside it blends: FX draws such a group straight into its parent, so the layer also blends with the layers below the group, where Premiere composites the nested sequence alone; export reports a Normal group without masks or a track matte that has a blended layer inside the same way, at the frames where its Opacity is full: a static 100, a key or held value of 100 in its range (including a value held from keys outside it), or a Linear value of 100 that the range meets. The report is conditional (the group passes the blends through at any frame, if there is one, where its Opacity is full and none of its effects isolates it) when only an upper bound reaches 100, a cubic Bézier ease's control values or a Linear value approached at the range's end, or when the group has an enabled effect other than a person or depth matte, Posterize Time or one of an unknown type, which FX renders nothing for on a group. Whether FX renders another effect can depend on its values at a frame (a blur of 0 renders nothing), which export does not decide, so this conservative report also covers a group that its effect isolates at every frame. Linear or Hold keys that stay below 100 report nothing. The reach is read from the keys, ease values and effect types; no Adobe case measures it. Before, every pair but (18, 0) omitted its clip, graphic or nest, and export wrote Normal. | **Measured** (`premiere_isolated_blend_codes_26_5`, a Premiere 26.5.1 save with VFC Version 9 / Component Version 7 records, its AME render): each code renders as its mode's W3C/Photoshop formula on encoded values at Opacity 100 over opaque chart patches (0.00–0.59 levels mean; linear light 6–70 levels worse, except the power-invariant Darken, Lighten, Multiply and Divide), Screen at Opacity 50 mixes in encoded values too, and parameter 2 alone selects the mode ((22, 0) and (1, 0) render as (22, 10) and (1, 5)); Premiere saves an (18, 10) clip at Opacity 100 with its default Opacity. A fresh conversion of the chart scores mean 0.991077 / minimum 0.984120 (25 samples at 0.25 s); 28 of its 30 slots differ from AME by 0.02–0.34 levels mean over 48 patches, and the Darker and Lighter Color slots by 9.97 (at most 179–181), so the case stays `structural_only`. `premiere_isolated_opacity_normal_static_50` ((22, 10) at Opacity 50 in a 26.3-layout record) scores 0.999878 / 0.999851 and is a `video_reference`; `premiere_isolated_opacity_blend_native_4_7` ((4, 7) at Opacity 50) still omits its upper clip, now for that adaptation's source span (0–2 s under a 1.5 s placement), so it cannot show the blend and stays `structural_only`. **Export gate, measured** (Oracle, candidate `8ad317133`; `tests/README.md`): Premiere 26.5.1 reopens an edited export of the chart (a Normal slot as Multiply, the (1, 0) probe as Classic Color Dodge, written (2, 11)) without a warning and reads its four sampled slots back as written; AME renders the 28 unedited slots as the pinned reference and the edited ones as Multiply and Color Dodge. | Premiere → FX: keyframe ids | Every imported key on the FX animation graph (Opacity, Rotation, Scale X/Y, Position X/Y, Anchor Point X/Y, audio volume and Linear Wipe guide keys) gets an id that names the layer owning its track, `premiere-{track}-{layer id}-{index}`. Layer ids are unique in the composition, including group children and every copy of a nested sequence, so the ids are unique there too, as the graph requires: one shared id fails the whole timeline. Premiere keys carry no ids, and export writes none. | Premiere → FX single-style text | Text (CR paragraph breaks become LF), PostScript font name, size, fill, tracking, all caps, justification, point/box frame and box vertical alignment are retained. A static Vector Motion is composed into the text transform and its keys with f64 rounding; a keyed one follows the graphic Vector Motion row. Disabled shadow/background settings, text selection and the generator in-point are not retained. An enabled background is reported as omitted (calibrated for caption cues only: the caption background box row); an enabled shadow follows the text-shadow rows. A static legacy UTF-16 JSON Source Text (the little-endian `u64` byte count of the UTF-16LE text, then `{"mTextParam": {...}, "mVersion": 1}`) imports in one bounded profile, with the Premiere 26 meanings of the same controls: every version 1 field present with its type and no other, every style one run from character 0, point text (`mWidth` and `mHeight` 0) laid out from its first baseline, `mAlignment` 0 or 2 as left or centred, `mLeading` 0, the PostScript `mFontName`, `mFontSize` in pixels and `mTracking`, and `mFillVisible` true with a gray `mFillColor` (equal channels, which no channel order changes) or false as no fill, with the stroke and the shadow off. Any other framing, version, field, type or value (another alignment, explicit leading, box text, several runs, right-to-left, vertical, Indic, Hindi-digit or ligature text, faux styles, caps, baseline, kerning or tsume options, a tab, a color beyond 24 bits, another fill color, an enabled stroke or shadow) and a legacy Source Text key or caption block omit the graphic with the reason; export writes the current document as the Premiere 26 payload, never the legacy bytes. Static legacy Text accepts only the complete transform IDs 2–21 layout (the later false Boolean ID 22 is absent) or the complete current layout, with identified parameter names and record kinds, default masking/edge/parent controls, and true Uniform Scale with Horizontal Scale at 100 or exactly equal to Scale. Legacy transform keys, nonuniform or unequal nondefault width stay unsupported; modern Text and Shape admission are unchanged. **Changed:** a legacy JSON Source Text omitted its graphic (`legacy UTF-16 JSON text from Premiere before 26 is unsupported`); its bounded static profile and equivalent uniform transform now import, and a legacy key or caption document is omitted as converting `only as a static graphic Source Text value`. | Native payload and structural tests cover the modeled fields.
