The pinned single-style point-text Adobe reference scores mean 0.997521 / minimum 0.997348 across nine Premiere/FX render frames. This does not prove box layout, strokes, arbitrary fonts or transforms. No universal pixel-error bound is established. See the text reference in `tests/README.md`. |
| Either direction: graphic text object keys (JRB-1990) | The text object's Position (14-field point keys with curved-segment spatial tangents), Scale, Rotation and Opacity keys ↔ the text layer's FX PositionX/Y, paired ScaleX/Y, Rotation and Opacity tracks, through the clip Motion key readers, writers and rules: the outgoing native mode becomes the next FX key's easing (Linear `0`, Hold `4`, and Bezier `5` as a cubic Bezier where it converts), key times round to signed milliseconds, colliding keys omit that property's animation, keys outside the trimmed placement are kept, and export rebuilds native keys from the current tracks. Keys use the generator clock: layer time = key time − `InPoint`. Export places each graphic at the generator in-point of its rate (one hour, rounded down to a frame) and writes keyed parameters with `IsTimeVarying` `true`, as Premiere 26.5.1 saves them. A static Vector Motion composes into the text keys: position keys and their spatial tangents move through it, Scale keys multiply and Rotation keys add. Export reports and omits a property that Premiere cannot hold (nonuniform or unpaired Scale, unpaired Position, a value outside the parameter's range: Scale 0–4000, Rotation −32768–32767, Opacity 0–100, or the Motion rules), and the static text exports. The former 4,096-key policies are removed from import and export; structural/value validation remains. **Changed (JRB-1990): Text Scale and Opacity, and Vector Motion Scale and Rotation (next row), now convert Bezier keys** in both directions with the Motion Bezier rules: the stored speeds are value per second, and the handles become a cubic Bezier easing and back. Import additionally accepts C1's independently measured Linear→Bezier Text Scale and Hold→Bezier Text Opacity. The in-handle is compared exactly, with no tolerance: only an influence of exactly zero or a speed exactly equal to the segment's average is neutral and converts, and anything else omits, however close. Import accepts C1's measured straight, tangent-free Bezier→Bezier Text/Vector Motion Position (speed in normalized Euclidean units per second), and Bezier→Bezier Text Rotation (stored degrees per second). Mixed Position/Rotation modes and curved temporal Bezier paths remain omitted. Export's Position and Text Rotation cubic easing restriction is unchanged: it reports the property and keeps its static value. its AME render (not pinned) draws B's ramp from 810.35 px over 300.03 px ending red, C's blue ring at radius 89.8 px (fitted stop 0.2998) about (810.5, 539.5), E's ramp from 660.43 px over 599.56 px, and the solid A and the video within the mean bound (render). **Export passed a scoped Adobe gate** for the approximated forms (`export_gate_approximations` in `premiere_isolated_gradient_fills_26_5`; Oracle, candidate `be9b6ed89`; the stop-merge fix's head exports the gate input identically after normalization; UI not inspected): Premiere 26.5.1 reopened an edited export (B under Rotation 30 / Scale 80, C with a red drop shadow, D with a third stop at 0.5 of opacity 0.5) with no warning and no offline media, read B's Scale and Rotation back, and one save kept all four Appearance payloads byte-identical, D's slot 31 = 0 included (Adobe reopen and save); its AME render draws B's ramp turned and scaled with the shape (29.989° over 240.33 px), C's red shadow under its fill and D as the fixture's D under its black stroke, the written middle opacity 0.5 rendering as 0.4956 against the fixture render's 0.4972 (render). **Unmeasured:** a nonzero y (omitted); gradient geometry under keys, Horizontal Scale or a Vector Motion, a shadow of nonzero size, a gradient shape's Opacity below 100 and more than three color stops, each converted with its warning; no Tesseract render of the gated forms is compared with Premiere's. Codec tests on the fixture's four payloads (D's opacity stops and its stroke saved without slot 31 included) and each fail-closed form, a mapper table with one row per approximation trigger in both directions and a table of opacity stops merged into FX stops (each check fails its rows when removed), a reader table of the transformed and shadowed forms, mapper round trips and the public fixture test (import, edit, export, reimport). |
| Either direction: text fonts (JRB-1986) | Import stores the Premiere PostScript name (empty style), except for an empty Text saved without a font (Inter Regular; see `Premiere → FX: empty Text objects`), and reports every font as unpackaged once; export writes a stored PostScript name or the packaged font's registry name; an unpackaged family/style omits the text. The converter never consults the bundled catalog. | No approximation: names are copied byte-for-byte, and a named variable instance without its own PostScript name omits the text instead of guessing. Synthetic tests cover per-font import warnings, registry matches by family/style, typographic and selection names, empty-style passthrough and omission; the pinned point-text project keeps `Arial-BoldMT` through Premiere → `.tsrct` → Premiere → `.tsrct`. |
| Either direction: text leading | Premiere adds leading to 1.2 × size; FX stores the full baseline distance. Explicit leading maps as `1.2 × size + leading` around Premiere's 32-bit automatic spacing. Automatic leading maps to FX automatic leading, which the FX renderer can raise for fonts whose minimum line height exceeds 1.2 em. | Native export narrows leading to f32. Automatic leading can be larger in the FX renderer by the font's metric excess. Structural tests only; the single-line point-text reference does not prove multiline spacing. No universal pixel-error bound is established. |
| Either direction: text stroke | Premiere draws width `w` outside the outline; FX draws a centered `2w` stroke under the fill. Stroke joins at sharp corners may differ. | Visible outline width exact by construction; corner shape unverified. Premiere render measurement only; no side-by-side FX render. |
| Either direction: static text shadow | One enabled Premiere shadow ↔ one FX `DropShadow` effect on the text layer, drawn under the fill and the text's own stroke and cast from both. Color is exact on import. The mapping is **Adobe-verified (AME calibration: 7 variants; residuals ≤0.07 px for blur and size, ≤0.1 px for the offset, ≤0.005 alpha)**: slots 12–16 are opacity, angle, distance, size and blur (each one-value copy changed only that property). The angle is in degrees clockwise from up toward the shadow and the distance in pixels, so offset = distance × (sin, −cos): the 45° shadow falls up-right, the 135° shadow down-right, and distances 24 and 48 fit 24.09 and 48.09 px. Blur is a Gaussian, `blurRadius` σ = 0.0966 px per unit: blur 8 and 24 fit 0.70 and 2.34 px beyond the 0.50 px edge of blur 0, and a box kernel fits worse. Size is the `spreadRadius` dilation, 0.489 px per unit: sizes 0, 2 and 8 fit 0.12, 1.03 and 4.01 px. Premiere blends a black shadow's opacity in linear light (exponent 2.4), so the color's alpha is 1 − (1 − opacity)^(1/2.4): opacities 50, 80 and 100 fit 0.246, 0.492 and 1.0. Still **INFERRED**: omitted slots read as Premiere's legacy defaults (color 0x3F3F3F, opacity 75, angle 135, distance 7, size 0, blur 40), and a non-black shadow uses the black calibration. Export inverts the same constants, writes every shadow value, including slot 13, rounds color to 8 bits and opacity, distance, size and blur to f32, and keeps angle 135° for a zero offset. Disabled shadow values are not retained. The FX renderer fades fill, stroke and shadow together with the layer opacity; Premiere is assumed to match (inferred). | Any wrong angle moves a shadow by at most 2 × distance. For the 42 corpus caption cues: the cues omit the angle, which reads as the inferred 135°; if that default is wrong the error is at most 2 × distance (6 px). Any other wrong omitted-slot default is **unbounded**. A non-black shadow is **unbounded**: only black was rendered, and Premiere's linear-light blend of another color depends on the paint under it, which one alpha cannot express. The calibration used one text, 150 px Arial Bold over red video; whether blur and size scale with the text size is unverified. Measured renderer differences, left to renderer follow-ups rather than compensated here: The FX renderer draws a text shadow's σ at 0.6–0.75 × a `blurRadius` of 1–4 px (piecewise across the tgfx blur levels), and halves and binarizes text spread, so any spread above 0 dilates about 2 px (spread 8 dilates 5.3 px). With the calibrated mapping the `premiere_isolated_text_shadow_stroke` reference scores mean 0.993710 / minimum 0.990500 (the pre-calibration mapping scored 0.992502 / 0.987429), but the standard floors also pass a no-shadow control (0.990331 / 0.985835; a no-text control scores 0.975266 / 0.969881), so the case stays `structural_only` with no score policy (see `tests/README.md`). Export color error ≤ 1/510 per channel. Evidence: payload decoding of pinned `practice_files_transcription_magic` (SHA-256 `a6aab608caafb9b19ef1900b815c04e3a6c0a32a4dd9d989527d9d8d430e28a1`) preview #41 and its cues, and of the disabled shadows of `2128_aiedit_en_na_multicreators` (SHA-256 `f093fb0eba054620a1cbcfff6f820d7261e06792d2e2a7a4bf11d2ff06e5830d`); synthetic payload, mapping and fixture round-trip tests, whose payloads this crate's encoder wrote; the fixture's AME reference (Asset `GfcGs8Oy3DoY00scG61t_vid`) and seven unpinned AME renders of one-value copies. No corpus Adobe render shows a text shadow. |
| Either direction: text shadow limits | Only the shadow is **omitted as a feature**, with a reason naming the value or rule; the text converts in both directions. Both directions: a shadow on scaled or rotated text (composed transform), on text with Scale or Rotation keys or under a keyed Vector Motion (graphic group) that scales or rotates (JRB-1990 combination rule: the text is scaled or rotated for part of its time), or on text without a fill (FX offsets the shadow in frame pixels and casts it from rendered paint; Premiere's behaviour there is unverified). Import: an enabled shadow whose opacity is outside 0–100, whose angle is not finite, or whose distance, size or blur is negative or not finite. Export: several, disabled, animated (JRB-1990) or non-normal-blend drop shadows; color channels outside 0–1; a non-finite offset; a distance, size or blur that overflows f32. A shadow-enabled flag other than 0 or 1 is malformed and omits the graphic. | No successful lossy conversion for these cases; synthetic omission tests. |
| FX → Premiere text effects other than the shadow | `Stroke` (none of `StrokeOutlineStyle`'s `enabled`, `position`, `blend_mode`, `color` or `width` is readable outside `fx_schema`), `InnerShadow`, `Satin`, `BevelEmboss`, glows, gradient overlay and every other text-layer effect are **omitted as features**, each named with its kind and effect id; the text exports without them. | No approximation; synthetic omission tests. |
| Either direction: box text first line | Premiere puts the first baseline at the box top plus the font ascent (about 0.72 em for Arial Bold); FX without an authored first baseline uses the box top plus the font size, and export omits a layer with an authored first baseline because Premiere recomputes it. Center/bottom alignment use the FX renderer's line-slot estimate. | First baseline differs by about (1 em − ascent), roughly 0.28 em for Arial Bold; center/bottom alignment can differ further by the line-slot estimate (**unbounded** in general). Premiere renders only. Measured once on the caption styles export gate: seven bottom-aligned 48 px boxes of 998 px (the FX renderer's last baseline 28.4 px above the box bottom) rendered 28.3 px lower than the caption fixture, for one and two lines, which fits Premiere placing the last baseline at the box bottom (INFERRED from the offset; no baseline was measured directly); the correction is uncalibrated. |
| FX → Premiere text paints and numbers | Opaque colors round to 8-bit channels; a translucent paint omits the layer. Sizes, tracking, leading, stroke width and box size narrow to 32-bit floats. A box offset moves into the anchor without moving pixels. Automatic vertical box alignment exports as top when leading is automatic; with explicit leading the layer is omitted. | At most 1/510 per color channel; f32 rounding for numeric fields. Synthetic export/reimport tests do not establish a general pixel-error bound. |
| FX → Premiere text masks and track matte (JRB-1990) | A text layer with masks or a track matte is **omitted** as an occurrence with the reason, as a root layer (`graphic was not exported: its text layer must have no masks`, or `… no track matte`) and as the only layer of a graphic group (`graphic group was not exported: …`); the other layers export. Graphic export writes neither, so the text would show where they hide it. **Output change:** a root text with masks or a track matte is now omitted; before, it exported unmasked, with only the masks or track matte reported as a feature. The matte source that a track matte consumes, which FX does not paint, is not consumed on export and still exports if it can (JRB-2023). | No approximation of the text. Unit export tests for a root text and a group's text, each with masks and with a track matte, keep a sibling text that exports and fail without the check. No repo-local case changes (byte-compare). |

| Either direction: clip effect stack order and bypass | Standard (non-intrinsic) clip effects keep their Premiere stack order, the order in which they apply. Premiere renders a `VideoComponentChain` in descending component `Index`: Index 0 renders last, and any materialized Motion/Opacity sit at the lowest Indexes and apply after the effects. Import reads that order and export writes the FX order reversed, through one mapping (`schema::chain_render_order`); component `ID`s record creation order and do not order the render. **Before** (JRB-1954, #4370), both directions read Index 0 as the first-applied effect, so every stack of two or more effects, a Crop or wipe included, was reversed; **after** (F24), stacks follow Premiere's render order. Omission messages count stack positions in that order (1 is the first effect applied); against main, the corpus shows such message changes in 14 chains of 4 cases, with the same omissions, and 3 two-blur chains, in 3 cases, whose converted order changes. Premiere `Bypass` `true` ↔ FX `enabled: false`; a disabled FX effect keeps its slot, id and values. Import constructs ordered `fx_schema::EffectRecord`s directly on the video layer, with `enabled` from bypass; export writes the current FX order, reversed into the chain as above, and enabled state (a pre-v13 effect without an id exports as enabled), with component IDs from 4 and the Motion writer's record generation (`VideoFilterComponent` 7, `Component` 5, parameters 9). Both writer choices are inferred: corpus chains start their standard effects at ID 3 (140 chains) or 4 (76), and every corpus blur is `VideoFilterComponent` 8 with `Component` 6. Native component IDs, record versions and Effect Controls UI state are not kept. An omitted effect is simply absent, so later effects then run on the output of earlier ones. Component `Index` values that disagree with document order omit that chain's effects as ambiguous. Supported intrinsic Opacity keeps its existing path; Vector Motion (`AE.ADBE Graphic Group`) next to effects still omits the whole occurrence, and standard effects on the sequence (`VideoTrackGroup`) chain still reject the sequence. | **No new loss** in order and bypass of mapped effects. Synthetic reader, writer and mapper tests (hand-written edited stacks export; written stacks reload through the reader), chain-shape rejection tests, and an edit, export and reimport of the derived fixture `premiere_isolated_effect_stack` (structural only; its AME video was a diagnostic mismatch while the Tint was omitted, its blur edges matching Tesseract's active-blur-only render at 0.9920–0.9987; the Tint converts since JRB-2001, see the Tint row). **Adobe evidence** for the order (Oracle run C6): in the F1 render (`premiere_isolated_stage_order_26_5`) a Crop or wipe at Index 0 with a blur at Index 1 renders sharp, so the blur applies first, and the swapped layout soft; a probe that swapped the component `ID`s rendered the same frames; the F1m render has Motion at Index 0 applying after the effects. Premiere reopens exported chains in the written Index order (gate G1, next row). Named F24 flips, expectations from those facts: `static_blur_stack_keeps_order_values_and_bypass`, `unknown_effect_between_blurs_keeps_the_remaining_stack_order`, `keying_and_masked_effects_omit_their_occurrence` (the `horror_title` Track Matte Key is now stack position 1), `converted_effects_keep_their_side_of_a_crop_or_wipe`, `converted_effects_on_both_sides_of_a_crop_are_omitted` (omission order), `omitted_effects_do_not_count_on_either_side_of_a_crop`, `flat_crop_or_linear_wipe_exports_its_effects_after_it`, `adobe_derived_stack_imports_order_bypass_and_its_tint` (until JRB-2001 `…_and_reports_the_unknown_effect`) and `edited_stack_order_and_bypass_survive_export_and_reimport`; slice 4's (JRB-2004) `adobe_keyed_blurs_import_as_editable_blurriness_tracks` (clip B of `premiere_isolated_gaussian_blur_keys_26_5` imports as [blur 10 (id 2), keyed (id 3)]; before, the reverse) and, test only with supervisor approval, `imported_blurriness_keys_target_schema_params_and_evaluate_as_premiere` in `crates/fx_composition/tests/premiere_keyframes.rs` (B's keys are on effect id 3; before, 2); slice 8's (JRB-2008) `corner_pin_keeps_its_bypass_and_order_around_a_blur` (a Corner Pin at Index 0 and a blur at Index 1 import as [blur, Corner Pin]; before, in Index order). |

Variants are supplementary mutations, not independent native witnesses. One-unit/pixel size, radius/falloff and transformed/nested halo extent are **uncalibrated approximations**. [Premiere documentation](https://helpx.adobe.com/premiere/desktop/add-video-effects/effects-and-transitions-library/stylize-effects.html) describes alpha-edge glow; [Elements documentation](https://helpx.adobe.com/ie/premiere-elements/desktop/special-effects-basics/effects-reference.html) describes matching controls, not Premiere numeric equivalence. Original sequence/media clock, native RGB/alpha comparison and Adobe edited-export readback remain **unmeasured**. |
| Either direction: Crop or Linear Wipe with standard effects (JRB-2077) | Converted effects keep their side of one non-default active Crop or supported cardinal Linear Wipe. Premiere applies the standard effects in stack order (descending chain `Index`, previous row), Crop and wipe among them, then Motion and Opacity; FX applies a layer's masks before its effects. Effects that apply after the mask stay on the one video layer; effects that apply before it stage the clip (next row). Export writes a flat clip's effects at lower Indexes than its Crop or wipe and a stage group's at higher Indexes. Converted effects on **both** sides of the mask are **omitted and reported** with `MASK_EFFECT_ORDER_REASON`, and the clip keeps its mask; an unmapped or failed effect is omitted where it stands and counts on neither side. A Crop at neutral values is no mask. A bypassed native Crop or wipe keeps its clip and is reported without applying the effect. Unsupported active wipes omit the occurrence through the existing wipe reader. Keyed Blurriness (JRB-2004) and a Corner Pin (JRB-2008) with its corner keys convert the same way, their keys on the video's effect (the stage group's video when staged), counted from the source In. **Before** (`main`), a blur or Corner Pin beside an active Crop or wipe was omitted with `MASK_EFFECT_ORDER_REASON`. | **Adobe evidence** for the order: Oracle run C6's F1 render (previous row). The F1 and F1m saves are the manifest cases `premiere_isolated_stage_order_26_5` and `premiere_isolated_stage_motion_26_5` (`structural_only`), which main omits clip by clip; their public tests check each clip's import, the written chains in the saved Index order and the reimport (`adobe_stage_order_fixture_converts_each_order_and_writes_it_back`, `adobe_stage_motion_fixture_keeps_motion_masks_and_keys_and_writes_them_back`). Their AME renders are pinned as long-term Assets (`LE8eVJuhbr3RDFRcej9D_vid`, `GHOw7TwGAH6i1EL7XBuS_vid`), and fresh conversions scored against them at the standard floors are below them (means 0.931972 and 0.964604), lowest on the blurred clips: each clip has AME's sharp or soft edge class, so the order holds in both renders, but the soft edges are narrower than AME's, so blur width is **unverified**. Export is verified in Adobe (Oracle gate G1, candidate `2bfe36b45`): Premiere 26.5.1 reopens the export of F1 with A's Crop Top and B's Blurriness edited, and of F1m unedited, with no warning, each chain in the written Index order and each value and key as written (25 readbacks within 2.6e-6); AME renders the reopened F1m RGB-identical to its C6 render in 240 of 240 frames, and F1 as edited (`export_gate` in each `comparison.json`). A blur beside uniform Scale, Rotation and Position renders with FX Blurriness times the mean world scale (renderer code); a blur with non-uniform Scale and Crop Edge Feather stay approximate. **Unbounded** visual error for effects omitted on both sides. The Corner Pin's place against the mask is **inferred** from renderer code (no render measured), and no imported corpus chain has one. Tests: `converted_effects_keep_their_side_of_a_crop_or_wipe`, `converted_effects_on_both_sides_of_a_crop_are_omitted`, `flat_crop_or_linear_wipe_exports_its_effects_after_it`, `keyed_effects_before_or_after_a_crop_or_keyed_wipe_import_export_and_reread` and `moved_linear_wipe_stages_and_omits_the_effects_below_it`; they replace main's `crop_with_blur_keeps_geometry_and_reports_unrepresentable_stack_order`, `linear_wipe_with_blur_keeps_geometry_and_reports_unrepresentable_stack_order`, `crop_exports_without_guessing_its_order_against_blur` and `linear_wipe_exports_without_guessing_its_order_against_blur`. |
| Either direction: clip processing stage (JRB-2077) | A Crop or wipe that applies after a converted effect (at a lower chain `Index` than it), or a wipe on a clip with nondefault static Motion or Motion keys, imports as a `GroupLayer` ("Premiere stage N") that takes the clip's range, Enable, Motion, Opacity, their keys and the mask. The video under it keeps its id, source range, playback, frame blending and the effects that apply before the mask; it and the guide are at the identity in the video's frame, on the group clock. A Directional Blur among those effects is **omitted and reported** in both directions ("a Directional Blur on a stage group is not converted"): no Adobe case verifies its map through the group's Motion (follow-up). Effects that apply after a wipe staged for Motion are **omitted and reported** (the group carries no effects). A Crop with no converted effect applied before it stays one video layer whose guide repeats the video's static transform and Position, Rotation and Scale keys: before, keyed Rotation or Scale dropped the Crop and showed the cropped content. Guide and mask ids are as for a flat clip, and the group takes the next id, so each staged clip shifts later ids by one. Export writes a stage group (one mask whose guide is one of its two children, the other a video) as **one clip**: Motion, Opacity, keys, Enable, range and the mask from the group; media, ranges, playback, frame blending and effects from the video; those effects apply before the mask and are written at higher chain Indexes than it. A group, video or guide field that one clip cannot carry (for example a video key, opacity, blend mode, corner radius, motion blur, mask or hidden flag; a group effect or key other than Position, Rotation, Scale and Opacity; a key on the staged Crop guide; or a mask that the F17 check rejects) **before** (6a) omitted the group whole ("stage group was not exported as one clip: …"); **after** (JRB-2077 step 3) the group exports as a nest (edited group row), whose rules omit what no nest carries with their reason ("group was not exported as a nested sequence: …": a Corner Radius or padding key, a group key that the placement cannot carry, such as a PositionZ key, a guide key or another mask that the F17 check rejects). A video whose range is not the group's still omits the group whole with 6a's reason: its nest would outlast its content, which rejects the whole export. Named flips (step 3): `stage_groups_with_a_field_one_clip_cannot_carry_are_omitted` → `stage_groups_that_one_clip_cannot_carry_export_as_nests`, the stage rows of `masks_that_one_crop_or_wipe_cannot_carry_omit_their_clip_or_stage`, the stage z row of `a_video_z_position_is_reported_as_not_exported` (still omitted, with the nest's reason) and the staged row of `media_of_a_clip_omitted_for_its_mask_is_not_inspected`. Neither the picture nor the sound of an omitted group is inspected, and the keys of the group and its guide are reported with the generic "animation on an omitted or unsupported layer was not exported". A group without that shape keeps the nest rules. Output changes: a stage video's embedded sound **before** was dropped on export with no omission (its sound was never inspected), and **after** exports as a flat clip's does, over the group's range; an invalid stage video (for example a legacy Media video without `sourceRange`) **before** was skipped by inspection and then rejected the export during conversion, and **after** rejects it during media inspection, with the same error. | The group's geometry composes to the flat mapping (code), and the staged Crop guide is the same source-pixel rectangle. Premiere renders the export of a staged clip as the save: the staged A and C (F1) and G (F1m) reopen with the written chains, and G's AME render is RGB-identical to its C6 render (G1, previous row); the Tesseract render's blur is narrower than AME's. Synthetic mapper tests (flat against staged documents, landscape and portrait media, speed, reverse, remap, nests, disabled clips), wrapper fallback tests for each named field, writer read-back of the written chain's `Index` order (Crop and wipe; wipes staged by an effect applied before them, by static Motion and by Motion keys), a public export of an audible staged Crop clip against its flat form (`stage_group_exports_the_sound_of_its_video_as_a_flat_clip_does`), and a public round trip from the pinned `feature_media_fit_crop_strict` Crop through one native clip back to a stage group. |
| Either direction: Crop and Linear Wipe with Motion | Premiere applies both in the clip frame before Motion; FX guides stay in the parent's space. Import: a flat Crop's guide repeats the video's static transform and converted Position, Anchor Point, Rotation and Scale keys, and a staged clip's group carries them; a Linear Wipe stays flat only on a canvas-sized source with default static Motion and no Motion keys, and nondefault static Motion or Motion keys stage it (clip processing stage row). **Before (#4465)**, import omitted a Crop as a **feature** when it imported Position, Rotation or Scale keys, and omitted the **occurrence** of a wipe on a moved or keyed clip (`Linear Wipe was not imported: static Motion moves the clip frame off the canvas`, `… Motion is animated`); a wipe on media that is not sequence-sized is now omitted by the stage classifier (row below; before, `Linear Wipe was not imported: the source frame differs from the canvas`). Unchanged: Motion keys that do not convert (retimed clip; unpaired, nonuniform or unsupported keys; each reported) leave static Motion, which the Crop guide shares, and a wipe on a retimed clip or whose keys cannot convert never becomes an opaque clip: import omits its occurrence (`Linear Wipe was not imported: …`). Export (F17 row): a Crop exports with its video's written Motion keys when its guide repeats them; a guide whose transform or keys differ, or a flat wipe on a video that does not map its frame onto the canvas unchanged, omits the clip (`masks cannot be exported: …; occurrence omitted`). **Before (#4465)**, export wrote no Crop when it wrote Motion keys (`Crop cannot follow animated Motion`), so the clip exported uncropped; a Crop guide with its own keys exported as a static Crop; and a flat wipe on a moved or keyed clip stopped the export (`Linear Wipe cannot be exported: …`). | Either direction: Track Matte Key (`AE.ADBE Legacy Key Track Matte`, JRB-2023) | Premiere keys a media or nest placement's source frame by one channel of another video track's output at the same sequence time, applied at the key's chain position like a Crop (standard effects at higher `Index` first, then the key, then Motion and Opacity), and does not draw the matte clip while it is consumed (**measured**, fixture G1: the matte region shows the fill with mean error 1.03 levels and no white matte rectangle; G1b: clip D's matte hid while its key was one this converter rejected, matte region → base 0.53 levels). So an active key consumes its matte track over the keyed clip's range whether or not the clip converts: the reader records every active key's matte track and range before it reads the placement (`claimed_matte_track_ids`, `consume_claimed_mattes`), and a matte clip over an omitted clip's range that no kept clip keys is **omitted** with the clip's record (`matte source of the omitted clip <record> was not converted: Premiere does not draw a track-matte source`); a clip omitted while it converted (a stage rule, a timing rule) drops its unconsumed matte layer the same way (`omit_unconsumed_mattes`). **Output change**: before, a rejected clip left its matte visible (the Oracle's candidate run left clip D's matte a root image with no consumer). The claim reads only the key's static Matte (`static_matte`), so a key whose Composite Using or Reverse is keyed, or whose record has another shape, still consumes its matte clip. Limit: a key whose Matte itself cannot be read (a keyed Matte) names no track, so its matte stays content while the clip is omitted with that reason. The record's Matte is the matte track's persistent `Track/ID`, not its `Index` (`horror_title` stores 7 for the track at `Index` 5 after a track deletion; the other 19 corpus records store `Index` + 1); import maps every kept track's ID to its position among the kept tracks (an omitted track leaves no gap, so the native `Index` would name the wrong track) before it reads any item and resolves the key to `PrTrackMatte { track_index, channel }`. Composite Using 0 (Matte Alpha) ↔ FX `trackMatte` mode `alpha`, 1 (Matte Luma) ↔ `luma`. The matte converts only when its track is strictly above the placement's and holds exactly one enabled item or nest spanning exactly the placement's range (D13-4: a superset or partial matte fails closed, because Premiere shows a matte clip outside the placement's range and FX never does), without a Crop, wipe, Opacity mask or key of its own; it converts as its ordinary layer (video, still, Color Matte rectangle, graphic or nest), its Blend Mode included, by **inference** (how Premiere's key reads a blended matte clip is unmeasured), and the FX `trackMatte` names that root layer. A clip at default static Motion with no converted effect applied before the key keeps the key on its own video or nest group (flat: FX evaluates a sibling source at its own canvas position, as Premiere keys the sequence-sized frame); any other clip stages (clip processing stage row) and the matte moves under the stage group as its direct child on the group clock, so the group's Motion moves the matte with the video, as Premiere's Motion moves the keyed picture (G5); effects applied before the key stay on the staged video (G7). Two clips keyed by one matte, alpha and luma (`corporate_slideshow` 1175/1176 on 1203), name the same layer. Export mirrors it (`CanonicalMask::TrackMatte`, the third arm of the one mask check): a video, nest group or stage group with a `trackMatte` and no masks whose source is a **video** that itself exports (blending normally: a blended source writes Normal with one warning, `unsupported blend mode was not exported (using normal)`, as the key over a blended matte clip is unmeasured) or a **still** whose coverage export keeps (`still::unsupported_matte_still`: Opacity 100, default Motion, Normal blend, contain fit, no input transform, time remap, effects, corner radius or keys; a written still carries its Opacity, Motion and their keys, but the key over a moved, faded or keyed still is unmeasured, and a matte's coverage must survive export whole) or a **static shape** that exports as the graphic of one Shape (`graphic::unsupported_matte_shape`: the graphic Shape rules, Normal blend, no masks, effects or keys; its rendered alpha is its fill and stroke coverage, as FX reads an alpha matte, by **inference** from the import row's graphic source and the still source's measured key, so a flat video or nest group only, not a stage group's child), beside the clip over its range (a nest's over the group's; a stage group's as the group's direct child over the group's range), not hidden and without a matte or masks of its own, and whose clip maps its sequence-sized frame onto the canvas unchanged (the Linear Wipe's check, `sequence_sized_layer_is_clip_frame`: a flat video or nest group at default static Motion without Motion keys, or a stage group, whose Motion moves the child source with the clip), writes the key in the corpus 12.x 7/5 record form (Matte = the matte track's written ID, `index + 1`; Composite Using and Reverse from the channel: `alpha` 0/false, `alphaInverted` 0/true, `luma` 1/false) and places the source on the lowest track strictly above every clip that keys it; a stage group's child source exports as a clone over the group's range beside the group's clip. FX's `mask_composite.wgsl` weights `(0.2126, 0.7152, 0.0722)` on encoded values, so a neutral matte is exact and a saturated colour matte differs by up to about 11 levels in the keyed fill (green). A Crop guide has square corners and exactly its video's transform and transform keys or, unkeyed beside an unkeyed video with both only translating (2D, Scale 100, Rotation 0, no skew or 3D rotation), another anchor and position, which move its rectangle into the video's frame by the difference of their offsets (position minus anchor); a translated rectangle outside the frame omits the clip with the Crop's validation reason, and a translated guide beside equal non-identity Scale, Rotation or keys stays out of scope and omits the clip; a wipe guide is a cardinal wipe, anchored and positioned at the frame edge that its angle names, with one Scale track of keys and no other key; a flat wipe's video maps its frame onto the canvas unchanged (anchor at the position, Scale 100, Rotation 0, no skew or 3D, sequence-sized, and no key on a transform property). | FX → Premiere: a video's key clock after a trim, move or input offset | **Changed.** A video's own keys (a flat clip's Motion and Opacity, its effect parameters and a Transform stage's tracks) export on the FX media animation clock: an affine mapping's authored input clock, which a trim or a move of the visible window does not reset and `inputOffsetMs` moves, or the source clock of an exact two-key unit TimeRemap. When the authored playback has unit rate, key time `t` exports at the source In minus the key time at the visible start, plus `t`; the clip's source In/Out, placement and selected frames do not change. A stage group's Motion and Opacity keys and a mask guide's keys (Crop, Opacity mask, Linear Wipe) still count from the clip start. Authored playback that is retimed, reversed or ramped keeps its existing rules and source In origin. **Before**, every key counted from the source In, so a trimmed window or an input offset moved the video's keys (Opacity 0 to 100 over key times 0 to 2 s on a window that plays source 1 to 3 s exported 0 instead of 50 at the clip start). FX holds an affine clock at an end of its authored input range that the visible window passes, while native media keeps running: a key beyond a held end **omits that property's keys** and keeps its static value ("a key lies outside the authored input range where FX holds the video's key clock"); a Transform stage with such a key exports as a nest. A flat Crop or Opacity mask guide that repeats its video's Motion keys **omits the clip** when the video's key clock does not read zero at the visible start, or when FX holds that clock before the last of those keys ("the Crop guide repeats its video's Motion keys on another clock"); a static guide is not affected, and a retimed clip keeps its static Crop. | **Exact** on supported unit-rate clips: key times keep millisecond precision, with no new rounding. **CPU coverage**: `video_keys_export_on_the_clock_that_animates_them` (with writer readback), `keys_past_the_input_range_that_fx_holds_omit_only_their_property`, `a_stage_keeps_group_and_guide_keys_on_the_clip_clock_and_video_keys_on_their_own`, `a_transform_stage_counts_its_videos_keys_from_the_videos_own_clock` and `a_keyed_crop_guide_off_its_videos_key_clock_omits_the_clip`. The FX contract is the runtime media animation clock (`TimeRemapChain::then_media_animation`) and its animator hold. **Bounded native export proof:** one unit-forward physical Video with `inputOffsetMs = 1000`, source 1000–3000 ms and Linear Opacity keys at 1000/2000 ms with values 20/100 preserved its editable keys through Premiere Pro 26.5.2 save/reopen and AME input normalization. The two-second 1920×1080, 30 fps AME and FX renders matched source labels and fade timing at 11 full-resolution samples (mean similarity 0.9973176363636364, minimum 0.995778). This covers affine Video-owned Opacity, not general alpha fidelity or every decoded frame. **Native coverage remains incomplete** for other Motion/effect owners, stage/guide and Transform clocks, held-domain static-value preservation and nest fallback, keyed Crop occurrence omission, and the exact-window unit-TimeRemap origin. Import is unchanged; reverse/nonunit sampling and linked-AE behavior are not proved by this case. Audio layer and embedded clip Volume keys still count from the source In (not changed; same clock question, found by code inspection, not by listening). |

| FX → Premiere: a flip, or a Scale or Rotation outside Motion's bounds, omits its clip | Premiere's Motion holds Scale 0 to 10000 and Rotation −32768 to 32767 (`MOTION_PARAMS`). A video clip, still, stage group or nest placement whose static FX Scale or Rotation, or any value that one of its constant or keyframe Scale or Rotation animations takes (one that export otherwise drops included), lies outside them is **omitted whole** with its keys: a key's value, and between two keys any value that a cubic Bézier easing overshoots them to (bounded as Levels keys are, `scalar_range`); a script's values are not known, and export reports the script as not exported. "flip (negative scale) was not exported" for any negative Scale, otherwise "static scale …", "Scale animation …", "static rotation …" or "Rotation animation exceeds Premiere's supported range". A keyed corner's `StartKeyframe` is ignored and its static FX value is its first key's. Key ids are `premiere-effect-{effect id}-{paramName}-{layer id}-{index}`. Only straight spatial paths convert (supervisor decision D-8a): each spatial control point (a key plus its tangent) must lie within 1e-9 of the clip frame of the segment to the next key. Between keys the check is exact when every moving corner is Linear or all share one Bezier easing between the same keys, because each turn of the quad is then a quadratic in one parameter. The pinned `premiere_isolated_effect_stack` render, whose Tint this slice converts, now **scores** 0.996817 / 0.991971 (its Tint segment 1.0000, where main's omission scored 0.7186) and is enrolled as a `video_reference` gate by supervisor decision under the standing authorization. Export is Adobe-verified on one edited document (static colours, a static and a keyed Amount with Linear and Hold keys, Linear Map White To keys): 150 record checks with 0 differences, a reopen in Premiere 26.5.1 without a warning that reads every colour, Amount, key and ten evaluated values back as written (72 checks, 0 differences; colours compared as the bridge's doubles, exact in the XML), and an AME render as edited, the unedited clip A 58 of 60 frames byte-identical to the fixture's render; a bypassed Tint, Hold and Bezier colour keys and Map Black To keys are not Adobe-verified (evidence in `premiere_isolated_tint`). Of the corpus's 169 Tint records (17 packages) 49 sit on media, 36 of them under a Luma Key; the rest are on nests, stills, graphics, black video, adjustment layers or Color Mattes, so, besides Tints on stills that the still image rows admit (not counted), only unmasked media Tints convert. |

Rendering: **exact by the shader** for the aligned subset on the clipped 8-bit input; the blended frames differ from Adobe by the unclipped-source gap of this source (E10: 1.94–2.89 levels against the clipped original), whose cause is **inferred** from the fit. Premiere 26.5.1 reopening the written form is measured: the export gate's four Transform records reopened as `AE.ADBE Geometry` with 12 parameters and their written values and keys (624/624 record checks, 181/181 readbacks). Round trip flips Uniform Scale on for equal axes without distinct tracks (render-identical). **Measured, not scored:** against the pinned E11 render (a long-term Asset, freshly resolved into an empty cache; the fixture is the Oracle's path-rebased copy of Premiere's save, the file AME rendered, converting byte-identically to the authored project) a fresh conversion scores 0.961480 mean / 0.850644 minimum at 5.000 s (clip F's first frame; A and F are the next row's approximations); the clips B/C/D/E score 0.9498–0.9904 (8 of 29 samples under 0.98; 20 of 41 in total with A's 8 and F's 4) while their pixels differ from Adobe's by 0.28–0.97 levels mean (p99 ≤ 23) and affine fits of the Tesseract frames agree with E11's fits of Adobe's within 0.0018 per coefficient, 0.36 px and 0.03° on all 17 fitted frames (control without the effect: 0.551476 / 0.362699). The geometry is Premiere's; the sub-floor similarity on sub-level residuals is the Ramp case's metric finding, INFERRED. No converter defect was identified; the case stays `structural_only`. |
| Either direction: approximated Transform parameters (conversion policy) | The Transform converts and only the parameter is approximated, with **one warning** each (Feature) that names it, the approximation and its error, on import and again on export. **Opacity** other than 100, static or keyed ↔ the staged video's `opacity` and `Opacity` track, under the group that carries the clip's Opacity, so the two multiply (supervisor decision D22b-2). **Motion blur** (the composition's shutter angle off, a Shutter Angle above 0) ↔ `motionBlur` on the staged video and the composition's one shutter at that angle with phase 0 (D22b-3): the first blurred Transform sets it, a later one asking for another angle blurs at it and is reported (`composition shutter set to N° by clip X; clip Y requested M°`), a keyed Shutter Angle converts as its first key's value; export writes the checkbox off and the composition's angle when the composition blurs within 0–360°, reports a nonzero shutter phase as lost, and otherwise keeps the checkbox on and reports the motion blur (D22b-10). **Sampling 1** (bicubic) → FX's bilinear sampling (D22b-4). Synthetic reader, mapper and export tests; the fixture's import, edit, export and reimport. |
| Either direction: unsupported Transform variants | Only the effect is **omitted and reported** (Feature); its clip and the other effects convert. Import: keyed Anchor Point, checkboxes or Sampling; Skew or Skew Axis keys (E11 measured a static skew only; Motion has no skew to export; D22-16); a bypassed Transform (Premiere renders without it; D22-5); a second active Transform on one clip, convertible or not (both omitted: the reader counts the active records before it drops the unconvertible ones; a bypassed one does not count; E11 measured one); a Transform with a Crop, Linear Wipe, Opacity mask or Track Matte Key (the mask or key keeps its stage; D22-6, D22-6a); media that is not sequence-sized (the frame Position is normalized to is unmeasured there; D22-4); effects that apply after the Transform (the stage carries no effects; D22-2); a value outside the native bounds; another parameter count, name or `ParameterID`; a masked record; adjustment, nest, graphic, still and Color Matte hosts (an adjustment layer's geometric transform does not render in FX; D22-8). `AE.ADBE Geometry2` (8 corpus records, `type_title`) converts only its positive uniform zoom with static Anchor Point and Position, as a Corner Pin whose corners land at Position + Scale / 100 × (corner − Anchor Point) (off-centre only on unrotated sequence-sized media, applying the Transform's measured E11 placement, which is unmeasured for Geometry2 itself); any other form is omitted and reported. Export: a mask-less one-video group exports as a Transform stage only under import's `Premiere stage ` name (a renamed or editor-authored group, and every Premiere nest of one moved clip, stays a nest; D22-18); such a stage falls to the nest path (D22-17) on Anchor Point, Skew, Skew Axis or 3D keys, 3D transform fields, a value outside the native bounds, or a video at the identity without keys (D22-14), where the nest's own reports name what its Motion cannot carry. | Premiere → FX: keying, wipe and unsupported masked effects | An **active unsupported** one omits the whole occurrence, which is **reported**, rather than converting the clip opaque without the effect. They match by a prefix of their match name or English display name, so a wrong entry only omits more clips: Radial Wipe by its observed match name (`AE.ADBE Radial Wipe`, 2 corpus records), legacy keyers other than supported Legacy Luma and Track Matte Key by the prefix `AE.ADBE Legacy Key ` inferred from its match name (an active Track Matte Key is the clip's mask, Track Matte Key row, JRB-2023; a bypassed one is reported like any bypassed keyer), and Ultra Key, Chroma Key, Color Key, Image Matte Key, Difference Matte, Non Red Key and Alpha Adjust by English display name because their match names are unobserved (Legacy Luma has an observed match name, JRB-2024 row); a localized project keeps such a clip and reports the keyer as an unknown effect. An effect with an unsupported mask also counts; the bounded editable effect-mask scope above is admitted (JRB-2125). A clip with a static Opacity mask (such a reference on its Opacity) converts (static Opacity mask row, JRB-2028); a supported **bypassed** masked effect retains its disabled effect and mask in the bounded scope. A malformed bypassed mask record omits that effect with its reason, not merely its mask. A **bypassed** one (`Bypass` `true`) keeps its clip, which Premiere renders without it, and is reported as an unknown bypassed effect, except supported Legacy Luma converts editably (JRB-2024 row); a missing `Bypass` reads as active (inferred) and an invalid one counts as active. Other Transition video effects, such as Gradient Wipe, are unobserved and not in the rule: they keep the clip and are reported as unknown effects. Wipe transitions between clips belong to JRB-1985. | **Unbounded** visual error (the clip is missing, or for an effect outside the rule the clip stays opaque). | Premiere → FX caption cues | Each cue becomes a root FX text layer (a cue with a background box, a group of one text: next row); wording (CR paragraph breaks become LF), timeline range, PostScript font, size, fill, stroke and shadow are kept as for single-style text, the stroke and shadow through the text stroke and static text shadow rows. Each cue is read from its own `FormattedTextData`; its track's `CaptionDataTemplateStyle` is only decoded, so that an unknown caption layout omits the track. Converted caption cues extend the document end like converted audio; AME behaviour for a caption past the last video item is unverified (JRB-1964 probes covered video items only). Placement is Adobe-verified (the pinned AME render's 1- and 2-line 48 px cues and 4 calibration renders: 72 px, TitleSafe 10/10, ActionSafe 20/20, 720×1280): the default-position corpus cues store no geometry, only center justification and bottom alignment, and Premiere draws such a cue with its last line's baseline at 95% of the frame height, centred, and earlier lines 120% of the size apart, whatever the size, the line count and the project's title-safe and action-safe margins. A cue becomes bottom-aligned box text whose lowest line slot has its baseline at 95% of the frame height (y 1026 on 1080p), pivoted on that baseline at the frame's centre; the box holds the whole line slots above it plus half a slot, so any line count up to its capacity ends on that baseline. A centred cue's box is 80% of the frame wide; a left-justified cue's box spans the middle 75% (x 240–1680 on 1920), **INFERRED** from two AME renders: one left cue's ink started at x 242 and one right cue's ended at x 1677. The wrap width is unverified: no fixture cue wraps. Paint order is Adobe-verified: every visible caption track is drawn above the video and the higher track Index paints over a lower one where cues overlap (the `premiere_isolated_caption_styles_26_5` render: of 777 pixels that C1's white and C7's stroke or shadow share, the overlap frame shows 0 white). Cues on a hidden track and disabled cues (`IsMuted=true`) import as hidden layers, read as for video clips (inferred). Right or full justification, another alignment, a text box, all caps, tracking, leading, a disabled fill and a stored position (document slot 33, whose sub-slots AME renders measured as linear frame offsets but whose base point is unknown) omit the cue by name: no render covers them. Caption track grouping survives only as layer names (`C1 caption 2`) and paint order; the language code, the generator in-point and a disabled shadow's stored values are not retained. | Placement residual **at most 0.65 px** over the calibration renders of `premiere_isolated_captions` derivatives (each changes one parameter; AME 2026 build 85, default Match Source preset): the last baseline measures y 1026.32–1026.65 on 1920×1080 for 48 and 72 px and one or two lines (rule: 1026), and 1216.03–1216.07 on 720×1280 (rule: 1216; a fixed 54 px margin would give 1226); TitleSafe 20→10 and ActionSafe 10→20 renders decode frame-for-frame identical (framemd5) to the default render (Asset `05KEehDuFJtJcKAyanY1_vid`). Caption size is verified on 1920×1080 only: Premiere scales caption size with the frame (the 48 px cues drew at 2/3 size on 720×1280, which is 720/1080 = 1280/1920, so a short-side, long-side or area rule cannot be told apart from 16:9 and 9:16 renders). On another canvas each cue keeps its stored size and its sequence reports one approximation (`caption size on a W×H canvas`); caption size scaling is part of JRB-1958. Rendered in Tesseract, the glyphs sit about 1.6 px above Adobe's: the model baseline is exactly 1026.0, and the renderer's glyph snapping of box text draws the flat-stem edge at about y 1024.8 against Adobe's 1026.4 (a renderer follow-up; the same render shifted 1.6 px down scores 0.9994448 mean / 0.9980063 minimum). Widths, line breaks and centring match; both caption tracks render, and overlapping cues are superimposed at one position in both. The resolution rule is Adobe-verified on fill and background only (a cue's own green fill draws green; a cue without a background over a background template draws no box; cues with their own background draw it); the other attributes follow the same rule by inference. The left margin is inferred from two cues, so a wrong margin moves a left cue by that error. Record shapes come from Premiere 25.5 (project Version 43: the 42 cues of the 29.97 fps `Practice-Sequence_CAPTIONS-ONLY` in `practice_files_transcription_magic`, whose template and cues share one style; manifest case `practice_files_transcription_magic_captions_only`) and from Premiere 26.5.1 (the `premiere_isolated_caption_styles_26_5` fixture's 7 cues and 3 templates, whose payloads use the same markers and which Premiere 26.5.1 saved byte-identical). Another caption layout omits every cue (fail-closed). No corpus caption track or cue is muted. No AME render shows a shadowed or stroked *corpus* caption; the fixture render draws the stroke and shadow template (C7, C3) and the fill override (C2); the pinned comparison scores 0.9961–0.9979 on those cues' samples (C7 0.9978, C2 0.9976, C3 0.9961), a measured distance, not a pass (caption background box row). Evidence: synthetic XML tests, the real corpus cue and template payloads, the `premiere_isolated_captions` fixture (25.5 caption records spliced into a 26.3 scaffold), whose AME comparison (0.9982659 mean / 0.9948304 minimum) stays a diagnostic mismatch (`structural_only`) until that renderer offset is fixed, and the `premiere_isolated_caption_styles_26_5` fixture (Premiere 26.5.1 save; its AME render is pinned and the fresh comparison measures 0.996546 mean / 0.987420 minimum, `structural_only`: the standard policy passes with each tested cue removed); see `tests/README.md`. |

| Either direction: sequence canvas of another size (custom canvas) | Import keeps a sequence's `FrameRect` as the document size: 1920×1080 or any other positive size at the origin (portrait, 720p, square, ultrawide, odd). Each placement's `FrameRect` must be the frame of the sequence that holds it, whatever its source's size (the Adobe-saved `vhsvertical` places a 1080p nest in its portrait sequence with the portrait frame); a placement with another frame is omitted alone. Media and stills keep their natural size: Anchor Point × the source size, Position × the canvas, nothing rescaled; graphic object positions are fractions of the canvas. A Color Matte or adjustment layer imports over the whole canvas, so one whose stream is another size omits its placement; a nest of another canvas is placed by its Motion (the nested placement rows). Export writes the document size as the sequence, placement and generator frames and each video keeps its source frame; an automatic AEP scope and its link take the same canvas (Automatic Premiere + AE packages). A zero, negative, overflowing, off-origin or malformed frame, or non-square sequence, placement or stream pixels, omit that sequence or placement. Premiere saves square pixels as `PixelAspectRatio` `1,1` or as any equal pair (`1280,1280`, `1920,1920`, `1000000,1000000`). An unequal pair, such as HDV `1920,1440` or DVCPRO HD `3,2`, is non-square, and its diagnostic names the ratio; a malformed pair is rejected. The export of the derived `premiere_isolated_vertical_canvas` import reopened in Premiere 26.5.1, and its AME render equals the native render at all 60 frames; at frame 0 both renders exceed the declared 3.0-level colour bound against the source media (4.0), a recorded failure accepted by a user exception for this root proof only ([fixture index](../../tests/README.md#vertical-canvas-premiere-2651-video-reference)). **Structural only:** the derived fixture in both directions (`derived_vertical_canvas_converts_at_its_own_size_in_both_directions`; its own pinned render is not compared) and synthetic canvas, matte, adjustment, graphic, nest, caption, staging, export-loss and AEP-scope/linked-import tests; no Adobe open or render of a custom-size Premiere + AE package was run. **Open:** caption size (caption cues row), a generator of another size, and Adobe evidence for a nest of another size (structural tests only). Script-baked normalized points fit to half a pixel of the longer side of their clip's frame (script animation row). |
| Premiere → FX timing: clip boundaries | Absolute native start and end ticks round to the nearest millisecond, ties forward, at all eight supported sequence rates. Each duration is the rounded end minus the rounded start, so adjacent clips stay adjacent. Keyframes stay clip-relative. The document does not keep the native sequence frame rate. Unlisted sequence rates are omitted, not approximated; retimed clips follow the retiming row. | At most **0.5 ms per boundary**; a duration changes by less than 1 ms, and a key moves at most 1 ms on the timeline. Synthetic rounding tests at every supported rate and synthetic mapper tests. Manual check: projects saved by Premiere Pro 26.5.1 at every supported rate converted again, and Chrome played the converted documents. |
| Premiere → FX timing: source range | Source-in rounds to the nearest millisecond, ties forward. At unit speed the source duration is the rounded active duration, so the clip keeps normal speed, and the native source-out is not kept separately; a forward unit-speed `OutPoint` up to one sequence frame from In plus the duration is stale and reads as that played end (synthetic structural tests; no Adobe-native fixture in this repository), while a larger difference still rejects; a constant-speed or reversed clip keeps its rounded source span, and a time-remapped clip spans the whole media (retiming row). | Source-in moves at most **0.5 ms**. Identical source-frame selection is **not** guaranteed. The rounded clip start and source-in, and the renderer's whole-millisecond frame times, can make a source request up to 1.5 ms early. That is past the decoder's 0.499 ms guard, so a frame can show the previous source frame (**at most one**). Measured against the pinned 24 fps Adobe cut: 12 of the next clip's 35 frames show the previous frame; the mixed-rate case matches on all 48 frames. |
| FX → Premiere timing: clip boundaries and document duration | Export writes a 30 fps sequence, or the rate that `--fps` selects; an imported sequence rate is not restored. Absolute layer start and end and a video-determined document duration snap to the nearest frame of the sequence rate, ties forward. Keyframes keep their clip-relative times and move with the clip. A layer that snaps to zero frames **rejects**, unless a group that exports as a nest holds it: that group, the smallest around it, is omitted instead (group as a nested sequence row). | At most **half a frame (16.7 ms at 30 fps) per boundary**; a duration changes by less than one frame (33.3 ms at 30 fps). Synthetic snapping, adjacency, and collapse tests. Manual Premiere Pro 26.5.1 reopen/render: every exported sequence was exactly 30 fps, shared cuts stayed shared, and a 200 ms gap rendered as six black frames. At `--fps 24` the Adobe-derived `premiere_isolated_rate_24_cut` returns to its frame-37 cut (CLI test); AME 26.5 renders of a probe at 23.976, 24, 25, 29.97 and 59.94 fps have the written rate, frame count and cuts. |
| FX → Premiere: sequence rate (`--fps`) | Each rate writes the `MZ.Sequence.VideoTimeDisplayFormat` that Premiere saves for it: 110, 100, 101, 102 (drop-frame), 104, 105, 106 and 108 for 23.976, 24, 25, 29.97, 30, 50, 59.94 and 60 fps. Another `--fps` value **rejects**. Stills, Color Mattes and graphics start one hour into their synthetic clock, rounded down to a frame. Generator clips keep `DefaultIsDropFrame` `false` and all media log `TimecodeFormat` 104 at every rate. | The display code, drop-frame preference and logging format set only Premiere's timecode display, and the reader ignores them; Adobe-saved generators use the drop-frame state and code of their rate. Codes from 196 Adobe-saved sequences in 26 corpus projects (`format/writer/sequence.rs`). No corpus sequence runs at 50 or 60 fps; their codes are the ones that Premiere Pro 26.5.1 saves in the sequence that it creates from a single 50 or 60 fps clip. Generator in-points from Adobe-saved projects at 24, 25, 29.97 and 30 fps, inferred at 23.976, 50, 59.94 and 60 fps. AME 26.5 renders at 23.976, 24, 25, 29.97 and 59.94 fps show the text, Color Matte and still on their first and last frames. At 50 and 60 fps, a two-trim export reopens and saves in Premiere Pro 26.5.1 with its rate, canvas and trims, and AME 26.5 renders it at the exact rate with exactly the selected source frames; against the uncut native render its pixels measure below the 0.99 / 0.98 floors (mean / worst frame 0.961975 / 0.943257 at 50 fps, 0.961358 / 0.946662 at 60 fps; cause not established), and its audio-marker onsets, like the uncut render's, are 20 ms later than in the source media. Premiere's timecode display was not inspected. |
| FX → Premiere timing: source range | Source-in stays exact in milliseconds and does not snap. At unit speed, source-out is source-in plus the snapped duration. A layer whose source duration differs from its active duration without `playback` or `source.timeRemap` **rejects** as a retime; retimed layers follow the retiming row. | Source-in is exact; the source duration changes with the snapped duration, by less than **one sequence frame (33.3 ms at 30 fps)**. Synthetic tests; Premiere 26.5.1 readback kept source-in at 0.117 and 0.464 s. |
| Either direction: video constant speed, reverse and Time Remapping | Import: a native constant `PlaybackSpeed` and `PlayBackwards` become two linear FX playback keys over the rounded active range, from the rounded source start to its end (reversed for reverse); a Time Remapping ramp becomes one FX playback key per native key (times rounded to milliseconds, easing as for Motion keys) on the full media clock, and a ramp that FX cannot hold omits its occurrence. Premiere plays the ramp at input In + speed × elapsed, so each key sits where the clip reaches its input, (input − In) / speed after the clip start, a time kept exact until it rounds once to the millisecond; keys of a curve from another In or speed that precede the parent clock's zero move later with the playback's `inputOffsetMs`. Only a forward, top-level physical video converts that clock from another In or speed, with In to Out matching the speed and covered by the keys; at unit speed In to Out must equal the placement's duration exactly. Such a clip also omits its occurrence when its saved In to Out passes the key before a last key at exactly the media end, whose segment is unmeasured, or when its rounded millisecond window, moved by `inputOffsetMs`, leaves its first key to that key (else its last key), and omits its effect parameter keys per feature, keeping their static values. The ramp's native Speed record (`TimeComponentParam`) reads as Version 8, which saves `IsTimeVarying`, `IsLocked`, `DiscontinuousInterpolate`, `ParameterControlType` and `RangeLocked`, or as the Premiere 26.5.1 Version 9, which omits them; another version, a Version 8 record missing a flag, or a saved flag with a value other than Version 8's still omits the occurrence. This is record parsing only. Export: a two-key linear playback covering the visible input window after `inputOffsetMs`, forward or reversed, writes native speed and reverse at rate = selected source span / frame-snapped active span. The original key values must stay within the source range; selected endpoints are evaluated exactly and must be distinct integral milliseconds. Inward trims and splits retain the original keys; their selected-window export has structural regression coverage, not Adobe fidelity proof; other bounded curves retain editable speed/Frame Hold segments with diagnosed interpolation and source-frame limits. Playback outside the supported subsets, and a `source.timeRemap` whose source range is longer or shorter than the active range, **omits its occurrence** ("time remapping was not exported: playback is outside the native speed, frame-hold and plateau-constrained ramp subsets"); **before**, both exported at unit speed in a source window that fits the media (`playback was not exported`, `time remap source duration was approximated with a constant-speed window`, or a shifted source start), so other frames played. Export: an exact FX hold (exactly two equal keys, the second Linear, containing the window after the signed input offset, on an instant of the half-open source selection before the media end) writes native explicit `FrameHold` mode 4 with `FrameHoldStart` and In at that tick and Out = In + placement duration, which may pass the media end; the key span, extrapolation and easing normalize to two Linear keys, and Motion, Opacity and effect keys and embedded sound are omitted with diagnostics. Adobe open/render evidence covers only two unanimated, unmasked holds, one with Out past the media end; animated, masked, looped or otherwise retimed holds are unverified. FX does not render `source.timeRemap`, so one over an equal span exports at unit speed with a report. Intrinsic keys on a retimed clip: import omits Motion and Opacity keys as **features** when the native rate is not 1 (the reader omits a time-remapped clip with Motion or Opacity keys except for the bounded Linear Rotation mapping below); export omits them when the rate it writes is not 1. Static values, a static Crop and the clip stay. A Linear Wipe on such a clip omits its **occurrence** on import, as on a time-remapped clip, and stops the export. Effect parameter keys are outside this rule, and their timing on a retimed clip is not verified. Sound of a retimed video is reported and not exported (audio rows). | Import rate error comes only from the millisecond boundary rounding (≤0.5 ms per boundary). The exported rate differs from the FX rate by the relative change of the frame snap (under one sequence frame over the clip duration). Native reverse and segmented playback remain approximations: source-frame selection can differ at interior sequence frames. Playback outside the native speed, Frame Hold, native ramp and segmented subsets omits its occurrence. Key omission is fail-closed except for the bounded forward-remapped Rotation mapping below; other property clocks under speed, reverse or remapping remain unverified. Evidence: `premiere_isolated_variable_speed_ramp` passes its AME comparison (`video_reference`, forward positive ramp only); `premiere_isolated_constant_reverse_0_905` stays `structural_only` (only pre-fix scores exist); synthetic keyed and cropped edits of the pinned reverse fixture, a speed edit of the pinned wipe fixture and synthetic export documents cover the key rules. Version 9 Speed parsing tests are structural (`native_version_9_time_remap_reads_all_nine_saved_keys`, `time_remap_flags_are_optional_only_in_version_9_and_still_checked_when_saved`); only the verified converter render of the trimmed placements below covers a Version 9 save. Speed with trimmed Time Remapping: `native_trimmed_speed_time_remap_keeps_both_placements_on_the_native_clock` asserts the editable curves of a Premiere 26.5.1 save of two trimmed 80% placements and the Adobe-measured source frames that they select, and `native_trimmed_speed_time_remap_edits_omit_only_their_placement` the reverse, speed-mismatch and media-end omissions on edits of that save; only the curve interior is measured, where a converter render of both placements is verified against their AME render, and the media-end segment, nested and linked clocks and the clock of effect keys are unmeasured. |
| FX → Premiere: nonlinear forward Speed ramps | At least four strictly increasing keys; each cubic has time handles at 1/3 and 2/3 and source handles fixed by adjacent Linear plateau slopes. Export writes native modes 6/7/8 and preserves the keys/source values. In/Out selects the covered window after the signed input offset; native speed accounts for the sequence-frame snap. Masks, matte dependencies and unsupported alpha effects stay excluded; animated properties keep static values with diagnostics and embedded sound is omitted. | Import is unchanged. Native saved-mode assertions and editable export tests cover the pinned ramp and an explicitly edited millisecond curve. Arbitrary handles, millisecond-rounded imports whose handles no longer match their plateaus, adjacent cubics, mixed holds/reversals, reverse ramps and keys at the declared media end stay unsupported, including invisible rounded endpoints that could exceed the packaged duration. Generated-project Adobe acceptance and render comparison remain unmeasured, including nested placements, Version 9 without an appended media-end key, and nonzero source origins. The crate reader omits nested ramps with nonzero In or nonunit speed. |
| Premiere → FX: Rotation on forward Time Remapping | Linear Rotation keys retain native media-clock times on an unmasked, top-level physical video with a strictly forward remap and no Motion stage. | Premiere → FX audio levels | Static source, clip, track, and master Volume and Mute, the clip's Clip Gain (`AudioClip/Gain`, linear) and its intrinsic clip Volume multiply into one linear layer volume; which stage held each level is lost. The clip Volume `Level` maps by layout: `[Mute, Level]` (Premiere 25/26, project versions 43 and 45) plays 0 dB at 0.177827939391 (10^(−15/20)), `[Bypass, Level]` (up to Premiere 13, versions 25–36) at 0.5; a Level without a value is 1.0 (+15 dB, +6.02 dB) and 0 is silence. A clip with Enable off (`ClipTrackItem/IsMuted`), a clip Volume with Mute on, a track with its timeline mute on (`Track/IsMuted`) or its fader Mute on, or a muted master imports at volume 0. A fader Mute reads Premiere's `true` or `false` or a number, on unless zero; a fader Volume and a clip Volume's Mute or Bypass still read only a number. Any other fader value is reported (`audio level not converted: …`) and its chain plays at unity. A Channel Volume at its layout's unity (0.177827939391 or 0.5 on every channel) converts; any other value or key on it is reported (`clip Channel Volume not converted`) and the clip plays without it. A bypassed clip Volume, an invalid value, a parameter that cannot be read or parameters in neither layout (before: reported as other audio processing), Bezier Level keys (none in the corpus), or Level keys under a disabled `IsTimeVarying` are reported (`clip Volume not converted: …`) and the clip plays without its Volume unless an independently read current Mute is on or a fully decoded static Level is exactly zero. Track, master and source automation and keyed Mute (static value used), pan, solo and insert effects are reported and dropped; audio transitions follow the audio transition rows. A broken or ambiguous link to one audio track or placement omits only that member; a broken panner, transition or master-track link still omits its whole track or audio group. | Levels are **exact**. Historical Tesseract renders of the converted cases are within 0.11 dB of AME per static segment (0.070 dB levels, 0.106 dB legacy), apart from mono (+3.00 dB) and the omitted Channel Volume (+6.01 dB). A crossfade extends the outgoing layer (active and source range) over its source handle to the transition end and starts the incoming layer at the transition start, from In minus the Alignment, each with a fade over the whole span: Premiere plays a one-sided fade as that half. A fade runs from silence at the clip edge to the level that the layer holds over it (its static volume, or Level keys that hold one value over it; a Level key at the fade's full-level edge shares the fade's key on the fade's millisecond), with inner keys at fixed fractions of the span in fade-in order (a fade-out mirrors them), rounded to the millisecond and valued on the curve: none for Constant Gain (one Linear segment), 0.441 for Exponential Fade, 0.013 and 0.193 for Constant Power. Other segments get the cubic easing (x1 = 1/3, x2 = 2/3) fitted between their keys as for Level keys. Level and fade keys share one track; touching fades share their full-level key. The fade-in curves of τ in [0, 1] are τ, sin²(π/2·τ^0.6457) (an empirical fit, not equal power) and (e^(3.5τ) − 1)/(e^3.5 − 1), whose 0.6457 and 3.5 are rounded fits to AME renders of the two pinned fixtures (run A5). A placement whose Level keys cannot import keeps zero gain without its fades (levels row). Fade edges round like clip boundaries (at most **0.5 ms**, **unwarned**), which that bound does not cover: near a fade's quiet edge, the shift can change the gain by more than the fit error. | Premiere → FX Custom Fade and short audio fades (JRB-2217) | One-sided Custom Fade, absent `FadeShapeType`, integer shape -23..=29 and default symmetry, maps to editable Volume keys of the approximate squared-sine power family. Incoming: `sin²(π/2·τ^(10^(-shape/100)))`; outgoing is its independently measured **time mirror**, not amplitude complement. Four incoming shapes and six outgoing shapes (-23/-19/-6/0/11/29 with fixed Clip Gain 0.5) measured; other parameters interpolated. Zero is squared sine, not linear. Custom crossfades, explicit types and out-of-range shapes remain contextual omissions preserving sound. Candidate-key notes can coexist with later clip-level failures. Exact tick-based short thresholds retain silence edges/cuts/handles and move the full-level edge inward to the sequence grid, constrained by clip/opposite fade/held Level. | Native saved/reopened controls with neutral provenance; Premiere 26.5.2 direct 10-second incoming and separate 15-second half-gain outgoing PCM, not AME/UI inspection. Outgoing cleanup/fresh READY verified; no fitted normalization/offset. Steady gain 0.4999635 vs 0.5; error ≤0.1341 dB above relative -40 dB / ≤1.252 dB above relative -60 dB. CPU native-anchor, attached-source, omission and tick-boundary assertions establish structure/envelopes, not full sound fidelity. Short 16.76 ms head becomes 59.3 ms, about 0.41 of full level at its original endpoint. Nonunit source clocks unintegrated. See [endpoint policy](../../crates/premiere_file/README.md#custom-fades-and-short-spans). |
| FX → Premiere Custom Fade automation (JRB-2217) | Ordinary editable clip Volume/Level keys with ordinary-fade recognition; never native Custom Fade replay. Unedited and gain-only-edited two-second incoming -23/-6/0 controls export a quiet **26 ms Constant Gain prefix plus Volume keys**; shape 29 uses Volume keys alone. Outgoing half-gain controls also export editable ordinary keys/fades. No Hold workaround, opaque state, media bake or generated script. | CPU `native_custom_fade_ins_export_unedited_and_gain_edits` and `native_outgoing_custom_shapes_compose_half_gain_and_export_edits` verify fresh import, ordinary structure and unedited/gain-edited full reimport envelopes within **0.27 dB above relative -60 dB**. Edge-silence envelopes/native edge fades provide an explicit local precision reference: attenuation lowers the comparison floor for export pieces and ordinary Level reimport; boosts never raise it. Unaffected Volume paths retain the absolute policy. Values/endpoints and bounded fitting remain unchanged. This is a numerical/editability result; generated-export Adobe opening/readback/render and full converter/native fidelity remain unmeasured. |
| Premiere → FX clip Volume keys | The source-clock `Level` keys become one editable FX `AudioVolume` track. Each native key keeps layer time key − In, with millisecond rounding; keys before In and after Out remain. Values are the native Level gain times the other static stages. Hold stays Hold. Premiere moves a Linear segment in fader position u = g^0.4475 up to unity and 2 − g^−0.4475 above, not in gain or dB. Cubic easing fits this curve before other stages scale it. Wide ramps are subdivided at millisecond midpoints in fader position, retaining the original anchors and adding at most 4096 keys. Fade-edge alignment runs before subdivision. Native handles and playhead `CurrentValue` are not kept. Rejected tracks, including coincident rounded keys or an excessive fit, are diagnosed and leave their sound at zero gain rather than playing through a silent interval. | Native key values stay **exact**. Each fitted piece is sampled against the fader model for **≤0.01 dB** error above −60 dB of effective gain. A piece that still exceeds this bound at 1 ms is retained with an approximation warning. This is a curve-fit bound, not a rendered-sound pass. The previous single-cubic fit could miss by over 1 dB on silence → +6 dB ramps. Independent PCM confirms that this fitting error is separate from the mixer's 100 Hz control sampling, which smooths Hold steps over up to 10 ms, and the linked source's 1024 unsignaled AAC priming samples (21.3 ms), which Premiere drops but the runtime keeps. Converter keys do not compensate for those engine/source-timing differences. Regression: `adobe_clip_volume_keys_stay_editable_through_export_and_reimport`; static-stage, Hold and timing-limit coverage: `refined_volume_keys_preserve_stages_holds_and_timing_limits`. Zero-gain rejection coverage remains structural only. |
| Either direction: MP4-family audio duration | Inspection takes the audio edit segment, rounded to the nearest whole sample, as the stream duration, even when the segment runs past the last AAC sample. A segment that starts past the last sample, an edit list with several segments, or a retimed segment omits the media. | **Exact** match with the native `AudioStream/Duration` on the 2 pinned and 12 local-corpus (`copy_and_paste_effects`) H.264/AAC files, whose 90 kHz movie clocks leave fractions of a sample; whether Premiere pads the excess with silence is not verified. |
| Either direction: WAV or MP3 file holding MP4 data | A `.wav` or `.mp3` file that the demuxer rejects and whose bytes start with an MP4 `ftyp` box is unsupported sound with a reason that names it (`audio source is MP4 container data, not RIFF/WAVE or MP3 audio`); import omits its placements and export reports its sound, as before, when the reason was the demuxer's `invalid audio media: end of stream`. The name replaces only the demuxer's rejection: a file it reads is admitted as before, and a RIFF header, whose little-endian size spells `ftyp` at 0x70797466 bytes, keeps the demuxer's verdict. A read error returned before or by the probe stays an I/O error; symphonia's marker scan ends on any read error and reports no suitable reader, an omission, as on main. The extension selects the demuxer; content never admits a file as another container. | **No new loss** (the reason only). Corpus `adobe-modern-speed`'s `Outro-boom.wav` is such a file (AAC-LC, `ftyp mp42`); read as M4A it would still fail its duration match, native `Duration` 543680 samples against its 545792-sample edit (2112 fewer). Synthetic tests: the pinned M4A under a `.wav` name; the stereo WAV with its RIFF size set to 0x70797466, admitted, and with its form changed, rejected with the demuxer's reason; a read that fails before the probe and one that fails inside a matched RIFF marker, both fatal. |

Export divides effective static/key gain by the same factor before the Level/Clip Gain split; FX mono unity writes native +3.0103 dB.
