Only those two layer gains change; the eight audible stereo fits are identical (at most 0.070 dB from AME) and the muted placement stays silent. **Export:** AME 26.5.2 renders a generated project with FX mono unity at **−0.000928 dB** on both channels, stereo unity at −0.000320 dB and a zero-gain clip as exact silence; Premiere opened, saved (Save As) and reopened it without repair, and the saved Levels differ only by float32 rounding. The current converter writes the same project after normalization of generated IDs, paths and modification state. General audio fidelity still **fails**: the Tesseract render has 477,184 samples per channel against AME's 480,000 (58.67 ms short) and a lag of −1,024 samples against AME's +1,024 (42.67 ms apart), the same without the factor. The export render's unused stereo-left channel is not exactly zero (peak −119.36 dBFS, RMS −137.83 dBFS); AME's render of the levels fixture also has a residual there. Adobe proof does not cover mono key interpolation, noncenter or other routes, transport playback or Effect Controls. CPU tests cover static/keyed gain, Clip Gain, stereo controls, unsupported routing and nested no-double normalization. |
| Either direction: bounded nested mono channel 0 (JRB-2217) | Import normalizes a nested channel-0 selector to its one direct true-mono leaf only with verified centered stereo routes, exact saved-track master inlet membership, consistent Solo/MutedBySolo, and static readable scalar stages without omitted processing. **Saved inner Solo/MutedBySolo propagation through the nest is an unmeasured assumption of this bounded editable approximation, not native audible proof.** Parent and leaf must be unit-forward, pitch-OFF, without variable TimeRemapping; informational pitch-OFF caveats are retained. Leaf onset/window/cuts and all static gains compose; leaf and parent each retain their 1/√2 center factor. Noncenter, unknown and automated routes remain separate unsupported routing. Rejections omit only the affected occurrence. Export writes current editable ordinary mono gain/trim, not the original bus, selection or solo structure. | Premiere → FX audio timing | Sound boundaries and source-in round as clip boundaries do. Source-out can pass the audio end by the 1.5 ms rounding allowance; sound has no final-frame hold. | FX → Premiere audio levels and volume keys | Each sound placement writes its level in the native clip Volume, as Premiere 26.5.1 does (`[Mute, Level]`: `Internal Volume Stereo` with `Internal Channel Volume Stereo` at unity, or `Internal Volume Mono`); every track fader stays at unity, where the level used to go. Effective native unity (mono 1/√2, stereo 1) writes the untouched default chain. A static Level holds at most +15 dB; Clip Gain takes the rest. `AudioVolume` keys become Level keys on the source clock (In plus layer time), each at its FX time and value. Hold stays Hold. A segment whose easing is the fit that import gives Premiere's Linear segment between the Levels that export writes stays one Linear segment; for mono sound, export predicts those Levels without the centered mono factor, up to 3.01 dB below them. Any other segment (FX Linear, another CubicBezier, a fit of Levels above 0 dB, or a fit that goes below −60 dB and whose Levels export or that mono prediction rescales, such as a fade from silence) becomes Linear pieces whose added keys lie on the FX curve and on the layer's 1 ms grid, until each piece is within 0.25 dB of the FX curve wherever the FX gain is at or above −60 dB, or is 1 ms long; such a 1 ms piece is kept and reported once per track (`volume curve approximated, not removed: …`). A track whose keys cannot convert is reported (`volume animation was not exported; sound kept at zero gain: …`); the sound keeps its placement and source at zero gain, without keys or fades, rather than playing its static volume through silent regions. This discards valid audible portions too; it does not implement negative gain. When the loudest key or the static value of a keyed volume is above 0 dB, that peak becomes Clip Gain and every written Level is at or below 0 dB, so Premiere shows the boost as Clip Gain rather than as Level. An audible video layer with volume keys exports its sound even without a static volume; volume keys inside a group are reported with the group's sound. | Values at the keys are **exact** (a Level rounds by at most one unit in the last place). Between keys, against the u-model: a recognized fit writes its native segment back, so Premiere plays the native curve, which differs from the FX easing by the import fit's error (previous row); every other piece is within **0.25 dB** of the FX curve above −60 dB. That check is sampled (64 curve parameters, the curve's turning points and each −60 dB crossing), not a proof for arbitrary cubics; tests confirm it on independent denser grids. A reported 1 ms piece is **not bounded**: 0.29 dB at 1–2 ms of a 1 s fade from silence; 24.69 dB in the first millisecond (below −40 dB) of a 100 ms fade from silence. Reimport fits each written piece again and adds that fit's error (≤ 0.25 dB between audible keys, ≤ 1.5 dB from or to silence; 1.09 dB measured). A written piece whose Levels stay below −60 dB reimports as FX Linear, so where Clip Gain lifts it above −60 dB, exporting the reimported project again can add keys there: the keys case's fade from silence to +6 dB gains one (layer time −394 ms, −59.8 dB) on its second export and none on the third to fifth. A 1 s FX Linear fade from silence to 0 dB writes 12 inner keys and reimports at 0.489 halfway (one Linear segment gave 0.192). Synthetic export-model, writer read-back, mapper and public round-trip tests cover Volume curves; the quota cleanup adds 4097-key preservation instead of the former refusal. Historical export gate of candidate `4ba9eb383`, which wrote every non-Hold easing as one Linear segment (`export_gate` in the levels and keys evidence): Premiere 26.5.1 reopened both exports with no warning text and read back all 11 static Levels and 12 keys, the Hold as a Hold (UI not inspected); AME renders the levels export sample-identical to the original project's render and the keys export within 0.003 dB of it, except the two fades whose native curve crosses 0 dB (80 ms −20 → +6 dB: up to +1.29 dB; silence → +6 dB: up to +1.43 dB). The historical export at `77c4f5b58` writes the same levels records, and the same keys records apart from those two fades, which get three inner Linear keys each (normalized byte comparison). Centered-mono normalization changes the current writer's mono records; that historical comparison does not establish its new output (the centered mono row has the static mono export evidence). An AME render of the revised export (`export.fixture_export` in the keys evidence) plays those pieces within 0.024 dB of the u-model and 0.268 dB of the FX curve (6 windows at the 80 ms fade's steep start exceed 0.25 dB), where the old export measured 1.47 and 2.27 dB; a diagnostic fit attributes the excess to Premiere applying the clip Volume about 2 samples late. Its authored 1 s fade from silence is measured only in its first 10 ms, where the source has signal: −0.52 dB from the FX curve in the 1–2 ms piece, then −0.25 to −0.16 dB; its midpoint is unmeasured there. On a continuous tone (`export.authored_fade` in the keys evidence), the same fade plays its midpoint −0.20 dB from the FX curve (within 0.25 dB at every candidate alignment) and −0.004 dB from the written curve, but not the whole fade within 0.25 dB: 25 of 199 windows exceed it, up to 0.48 dB in the first 10 ms, which depend on the alignment. An AME render of a static +20 dB export (Level 1.0 plus Clip Gain 1.778; `export.static_plus20db`) plays within 0.001 dB of +20 dB. The converter at `77c4f5b58` exports the same native projects for these three renders after normalization. |

An audio layer whose `playback` makes its source duration differ from its active duration is omitted as an occurrence (`retimed audio layer was not exported`) and the export continues; a duration-preserving `playback` exports at unit speed with a `playback settings` report; without `playback` the mismatch rejects. | The picture can move up to **half a frame (16.7 ms at 30 fps)** relative to its own sound. A placement is omitted, and its siblings convert, when its master clip's chain holds an active Crop, Linear Wipe or Track Matte Key, an intrinsic component, a coverage effect or an effect mask (each would hide or move part of the picture), or an active Transform, or a Geometry2 that does not convert (only its centered positive zoom converts; read with its own parameters, the saved Rotation marker included), that can hide the picture at some source time, by an Opacity or rendered Scale (Scale Height on both axes under Uniform Scale) that reaches 0, its Bezier easing included, by a Position, Anchor Point and Scale that can move the whole picture out of the clip's frame (keys bounded by their extremes; under a Rotation or Skew, whose composition with Scale is unmeasured, any Anchor Point off the picture or Position not inside the frame counts), or by moving, scaling or turning the picture beside another active Transform or Geometry2 that does so, a Corner Pin or a Mosaic, since their combined geometry is not evaluated (fail-closed: such a chain is omitted even where Premiere shows part of the picture, as the Transform fixture's own Position 0.75:0.5 before the source save's inward Corner Pin likely does), or whose values cannot be read (import converts no source Transform, so the picture would show where it hides it; **Changed:** one at Scale 0 or with a Position that moved the picture out of its frame converted without it and showed the picture, and so did two Transforms that each keep part of it in the frame but together move it out, a moved Transform beside another such effect, and a Geometry2 that hides the picture and does not convert), when the chain is not a `VideoComponentChain`, lacks its `ComponentChain` or names a missing component, or when it is the placement's own chain. Two `VideoComponentChain` children are a repeated declared field, so such a master clip does not decode wherever a master clip is read (video and nested placements, a sound's source gain, the caption and graphic master checks): each of its placements, sounds or cues is omitted (`duplicate field VideoComponentChain`) and the other placements convert. | **Linked editing is lost:** each placement's copy edits alone, reported once per master clip (`linked editing of the source effects is not converted`). Converts without its source effects, each reported: a source Transform, or a Geometry2 that does not convert, that leaves the picture where it is or keeps part of it in the clip's frame with no other active effect that changes which part shows, or a bypassed one (FX has no transform effect; the placement's own one Transform still stages; the picture shows without the Transform's geometry; whether the placement's Motion then moves that part off the canvas is not evaluated, and the check treats the picture as covering its whole frame, not its own transparency), a placement whose own effects follow its Crop, Linear Wipe or Track Matte Key (one FX mask keeps one side in order), a placement that cannot stage them (a shared or retimed matte), and still images, Color Mattes, adjustment layers and nest placements (`VideoComponentChain not converted`). Source keys on a retimed placement keep static values. A source Corner Pin whose one keyed corner moves on a curved spatial path along Linear keys, the saved form (the reader admits it only with the key flags that Premiere saved: temporal flags 0 and automatic spatial handles, mode 5 with flags 4), converts per placement as at most 64 paired straight Linear keys on points of the path, the saved keys among them and off-trim keys kept, reported as an approximation with its key count and bound (**approximated**): each chord stays within 0.2 source pixels of the path (a halving of each interval certified by control-polygon and chord length bounds), and with millisecond key times, FX's millisecond clock and the rounding of the f32 pixel corners that FX draws the corner stays within 0.5 source pixels at every time of the hypothesized traversal, a constant speed along the saved path in normalized frame coordinates, which the save's key speeds and 1 s readback support and no Adobe render measures. The certificate holds over a numerical domain that is checked before any work: frame sides of 1 to 8192 pixels, every corner, key and spatial control point within ±4 of the frame's origin, every key within 2^53 ticks of the source In; its pieces are counted before any is built. The quad is certified convex along the whole path by the turns at every control point of each part; the straight keys must keep the quad that FX draws convex through the rounding of its f32 pixel corners, and pass the existing quad check. Premiere's spatial tangents and their automatic recomputation are lost, and an edited key leaves the bound. Another curved form (other key flags, a second keyed corner, a Hold or Bezier key, more than 64 keys, more than 4 curved intervals, a frame, value or key time outside the numerical domain, a part that 2^12 pieces per interval cannot bound, a quad not certified convex or one that FX may draw flat, colliding millisecond keys, a retimed placement's keys) keeps the Corner Pin omitted or static with its reason, and its siblings convert. A source Directional Blur, Ramp or Mosaic follows the frame rules of its picture, flat or staged, and a source Corner Pin, Mosaic or Blur that repeats edge pixels is omitted on a linked composition of another size than the canvas, as the clip's own is. A stack none of whose effects converts is reported as not converted, without the linked-editing report; on a masked placement it still stages the mask. The pinned `premiere_isolated_source_effects_26_5` imports its Corner Pin (3 saved Upper Left keys as 40 straight keys, certified within 0.404 source pixels; an independent dense traversal, evaluated as FX draws it at every millisecond within 1 ms, finds at most 0.215) and its bypassed Blur on each placement, and exports and reimports an edited copy. **Adobe evidence:** a historical sampled import comparison against the pinned AME render (Asset `bYD7yrX1kuB039I71qFE_vid`) passed; it does not establish the fidelity of the current converter. The native render of an edited export **fails** its comparison on the Blur and Corner Pin: Blur strength and the bottom/right edges differ from Adobe. The case is not a strict `video_reference` case. The traversal is a hypothesis until native evidence measures it; source readbacks at frames 75 and 119 remain unmeasured. | Premiere → FX: Source Graphic placements (a graphic master clip's own `VideoComponentChain`) | A graphic is a Source Graphic when its master clip owns a `VideoComponentChain` and the master's `VideoClip` plays the placement's generator source (the existing source-identity check); a shared master or binary hash alone does not make one. The master's chain holds the objects that every placement shows, and a placement's own chain may hold only its clip Motion and clip Opacity. Each placement reads the shared chain afresh through the existing object, Vector Motion and clip Opacity readers and keeps its own range, source In (the pinned save: original placement 0 s, I1 3600 s, I2 1 s) and **static** clip Motion, so every placement imports its own copy with its own layer ids. The loss of shared editing is reported once per master (`Source Graphic shared editing is not converted: …`). A nondefault clip Motion becomes a group (`Premiere graphic Motion N`) around the complete root that the graphic import makes, with the placement's range and visibility, so the Motion moves the whole graphic once, mapped as a media clip's Motion of a canvas-sized picture; the root keeps its clip Opacity, blend mode and clock. Each of these omits only its placement: a clip Opacity mask on shared Source Graphic content (its mask frame is unmeasured, even at default clip Motion; ordinary graphics keep their measured static mask mapping), keyed clip Motion (unmeasured on graphics), keys in the shared chain (clock unmeasured), any other component in a placement's chain, a second clip Opacity (`duplicate intrinsic Opacity`, which the Opacity reader would not check), a component reference to another record type (its graph error), `DefaultMotion` or `DefaultOpacity` on the shared chain, and the existing mask, bypass and source-identity guards. As a Track Matte source, a graphic of several objects, a Source Graphic or an ordinary one, is keyed as its whole root group, which a flat keyed clip names beside it and a staged one moves under its stage group; before, the key named the first object, and a staged key aborted the conversion (`the matte layer is not beside its clip`). A graphic whose master has no chain keeps the ordinary rules, which still omit nondefault clip Motion. **Before:** every placement of the pinned save was omitted (clip Motion on I1 and I2; no objects on the original placement's own chain). | **Lost:** shared editing: each copy edits alone. Structural evidence only: public import tests of both sequences of the untouched pinned save (`premiere_isolated_source_graphic_26_5`), and supplementary XML-edited controls, among them a video keyed by I2 at the default Motion, staged and flat. **Adobe evidence:** a historical sampled import comparison against the pinned AME render (Asset `MSGcmi8BRqSrYfaP4G0w_vid`) passed; it does not establish the fidelity of the current converter. The native render of an edited export **fails** its comparison: the white `NEW` text misses the foreground check, and a lossless comparison fails too, so encoding alone does not explain it; its cause is unresolved. The case is not a strict `video_reference` case. The resolved font binary and animated shared-text clocks are not established. **Export unchanged:** a Motion group exports as a nested sequence whose placement carries the Motion, which reads back as a group with that Motion around its own editable copy, clipped to its frame (`Premiere → FX: unsupported nested placements (JRB-1979)`); a graphic matte source is not exported, so its keyed clip is omitted; an empty Text exports only in a packaged font (next row). |

its fill while enabled, with its fill paint: its solid colour, or a linear or radial gradient on the layer's x axis in the gradient row's form, with that row's warnings (opacity stops, a scaled or rotated rectangle, a kept shadow, Opacity below 100, more than three stops); its stroke while enabled, coloured and wider than 0 with its join and miter limit (a stored but disabled fill or stroke is ignored, and so is an undashed stroke's dash offset, as FX draws them); its static Position, Anchor Point, Scale, Horizontal Scale, Rotation and Opacity; a drop shadow and the other layer fields follow the graphic Shapes row. **Approximated** with one warning (`rectangle gradient fill <kind> approximated by its solid fill colour: <reason>; colours differ from the gradient's stops by up to N/255`): a gradient that the Shape rules do not export (reflected or conic, a nonzero y or an axis under 1e-4 px; Another profile name or type, an extra field, and the 8-bit sequence profile with data but no peak luminance (Premiere 25.2 and 25.5) still reject with the existing messages, and the short 8-bit profile still rejects on a source. The 26.5.1-upgraded `adobe-cinemagraph` and `adobe-improve-audio` now convert, each to a project document byte-identical to this branch's conversion of its legacy file. | Premiere → FX: repeated identical `RelativePath` (JRB-2082) | Premiere 25.0 and 26.5.1 saves can write a Media's `RelativePath` twice with the same text. Other strings stay separate: `./a.mp4` and `a.mp4` are two package-local paths and still reject, and missing, empty or absolute paths still reject with the existing messages. | **No new loss**: an identical copy carries no information. With the colour forms, that as-saved 26.5.1 project converts to a project byte-identical to `main`'s conversion of its sanitized copy. |
| Premiere → FX: media paths saved on Windows | When every `FilePath`/`ActualMediaFilePath` of a Media is a Windows drive-absolute path (`C:\…`) that the host does not treat as absolute, Premiere saved it on Windows: those paths name files on the saving machine, so they are recognized but never opened or used as candidates. Its `RelativePath` separators `\` become `/`; the package-local hint must then resolve inside the package and pass the existing hash and containment checks. Such a Media rejects when it has no package-local hint (only `..\…`), because no alias can verify outside media, or when a hint names a drive. On a POSIX host, drive-relative (`C:name`), UNC, device (`\\?\…`) and mixed Windows/POSIX aliases still reject with the existing message, and every other Media keeps `\` as a file-name character; a Windows host keeps its unchanged native path handling. | **No new loss**: the media is the package file, byte-for-byte, hashed like any other hint; the missing hint of a single-hint record, the only corpus form, stays a missing-media omission, and a missing second hint beside a live package-local one is stale history (next row). Unverified: names whose case differs from the file (they resolve only on a case-insensitive host), and three corpus `motionarray-83972` hints that Premiere wrote with an escaped `&amp;`, which stay missing. Synthetic reader and resolution tests with that corpus project's record shape; the pinned audio clips and disabled layers fixtures, with only their paths in the Windows form, read the same placements, source ranges, gains and disabled clips. |
| Premiere → FX: stale relative path hints | A Media can keep a `../` `RelativePath` to the folder it was imported from beside its package-local `./` hint, with absolute aliases on a volume that is gone (corpus `adobe-modern-speed`: `../04 Captions/Footage/Intro-Boom.wav` beside `./Footage/Intro-Boom.wav`). A live package-local hint identifies the media by itself: other relative hints that are missing are ignored as stale history, and every live candidate must still hold the same bytes. Without one, a missing hint needs a live absolute alias with the same bytes; otherwise the media is missing and its placements are omitted (`missing media: native media candidate is missing; …`). A record whose relative hints are all live but outside the package, without a live alias, still rejects. | **No new loss**: the media is the package-local file, byte-for-byte; nothing is relinked by name or searched for, and the ignored hints are not reported. **Before**, the missing hint stopped the whole conversion; `adobe-modern-speed` now converts its sound (local run, media not committed). Synthetic resolution tests. |
| FX → Premiere: Eye Contact replacement (`source.eyeContact`) | Enabled: the clip references the active Eye Contact output (`VideoSource::active_asset_id`), packaged byte-for-byte; the original asset and the toggle are lost and reported as an omission. Embedded sound of an enabled Eye Contact clip is omitted: the original is not packaged and replacement audio is unverified; other layers keep the audio mapping. | FX → Premiere: audio enhancement (`source.enhancement` of an audio layer, JRB-1949) | Enabled: the sound placement references the enhanced output (`AudioSource::active_asset_id`), packaged byte-for-byte with its own channel layout; the original asset and the toggle are reported as not exported. Disabled: the original exports and the unused output is reported with the toggle. Only the active asset is inspected, packaged and placed; the inactive one is never read, so it may be damaged or absent from the archive. A missing or damaged active output still stops the export, unsupported active sound omits the placement, and a `sourceIntrinsicDuration` mismatch rejects and names the output: none falls back to the original. Placements that share the active asset share one packaged file and keep their own ranges. Reimport yields a plain audio layer of the exported file. A video's `audioEnhancement` is unchanged: its embedded sound still exports the original's sound and the enhancement is reported. | Active source choice matches the renderer; bytes are unchanged, not a claim of audible equality. The toggle and the lineage are lost completely. Public export tests `audio_enhancement_exports_only_the_asset_that_plays`, `inactive_audio_enhancement_asset_is_never_read` and `unusable_active_audio_enhancement_output_never_falls_back_to_the_original`. Adobe: a five-second standalone-audio enabled/disabled selection export opened/saved in Premiere, retaining selected media, trims, stereo and gain; its rendered audio is sample-identical to the unchanged independent native reference. Both retain a +1024-sample (~21.33 ms) delay relative to source PCM: no absolute-fidelity or Tesseract-render equality claim. Sampled black-canvas checks are not audio proof. |
| Premiere ↔ FX: explicit Frame Hold child in a trimmed nest | A physical-video mode-4 hold already admitted by the native reader retains its source In and held instant through a unit-forward, matching-rate outer window. Only the hold's input duration shortens. Repeated copies share media but have independent editable constant playback keys; current edits export through ordinary native Frame Hold records inside separate nests. Own-effect keys that would silently freeze on the wrong held clock omit that child with nest/track/occurrence context and preserve siblings. Alpha Glow, Transform and Posterize Time retain their existing diagnosed static/omission rules; top-level hold policy is unchanged. | Premiere → FX: nested sequence placement (JRB-1979) | Becomes a `GroupLayer` over the placement's range holding an independent copy of the part of the inner timeline that the placement shows, on the group clock (a retimed placement, or one whose sequence has another frame rate, keeps its inner clock: next row); the placement's Blend Mode is the group's (Opacity Blend Mode row). The inner black canvas is not copied, so inner gaps are transparent; AME renders them transparent too (outer 9–10 s of the fixture shows the red outer clip; AME render of the derived fixture, not a Premiere-saved nest). If Premiere shows black there, every inner gap inside a placement shows the lower outer tracks instead, for the whole gap. At normal speed, inner content outside the placement's window is **not** copied, so lengthening the group later reveals nothing. A retimed nest keeps every inner placement that overlaps its window, untrimmed, and drops only those wholly outside it, so lengthening its mapping can reveal that kept content. Repeated placements become unlinked copies: an edit to one no longer changes the others. The inner sequence survives only as the group name; its GUID and native master/source records are lost. All copies keep the shared media assets. Inner sound, stills, Color Mattes and graphics follow the nests-with-audio row below; embedded video sound inside a nest is reported on export. Other non-video track items are reported by the inner sequence's own read. | Each copied boundary rounds its absolute outer time to the nearest millisecond, as at top level: at most **0.5 ms per boundary**, no accumulation across levels. Synthetic reader/mapper tests plus a structural test of `feature_nested_sequence_strict`, which is **XML-edited** and scored against its AME render (`proof: video_reference`, Asset `eSTtvtw08unRUkVzj8Qs_vid`): a structural derivative, not claimed to have been authored through Premiere's UI; Premiere UI controls were not inspected. Its nest records are edits of `feature_nested_second_sequence_strict`, whose own nest was an edit too, so Premiere has never saved this shape. The `food_lower_third` corpus nest, at a nondefault Position, shows only graphics with a nondefault clip Motion, which an ordinary graphic does not convert. This effect handling does not establish an end-to-end corpus conversion or render-fidelity pass. |
| Premiere → FX: unsupported nested placements (JRB-1979) | A nest with reverse playback or an unsupported time remap, a speed that its saved window does not confirm, off-frame in/out at normal speed and the outer rate, a Track Matte Key on another canvas, an out point past the end of its inner sequence, Opacity keys on a retimed nest or that do not convert, such as two native keys within one millisecond (their static value could show what they hide), Motion beside a Track Matte Key, off-frame start/end, or a master clip that plays another source is omitted and reported, never approximated. Occurrence effects follow the [bounded nested occurrence effect rules](../../crates/premiere_file/README.md#nested-occurrence-effects), not a blanket active-effect rejection. A forward constant-speed nest whose saved In/Out its speed confirms (source time over placement time, the media rule, whatever the inner frame rate) becomes a group whose playback maps the placement linearly onto that window, each end rounded once; its inner placements keep their inner-clock places, a retimed nest inside a normal-speed nest keeps its rate, its Frame Blending/Optical Flow and Motion keys are reported and not converted (static values kept), it has no sound, and export omits the group. A normal-speed nest whose sequence has another frame rate plays on its inner clock the same way, at unit rate from In, with its Motion keys on that clock and its audio item paired; export omits its group unless the window starts at inner zero, where the clock is plain and the group exports as a plain nest at the export rate, its inner boundaries snapped to export frames as at top level (an inner clip that collapses there omits the smallest nest around it). A nest of another canvas is placed by its Motion over the inner canvas (Anchor Point × inner canvas, Position × outer), even at default Motion, and clipped to the inner canvas; export writes that group as an outer-canvas nest cropped to the inner canvas only when it fits the outer one, without restoring the inner size, and otherwise omits it with the Crop-range reason. So is a placement of a timeline on a nesting cycle (audio-track placements count toward a cycle, so a cycle that runs only through audio also omits the video placements of its timelines: a deliberate over-rejection), or one more than 8 levels deep: each costs that placement, not the timeline. The former expanded-layer and per-read byte quotas are removed (see the capacity inventory). A nest's static or keyed Motion without a Track Matte Key becomes its group's transform and keys, the static value of a keyed property its first key's (Premiere shows that value before a later first key), and the group is clipped to the nested frame by a child guide in the nest-Crop form (formerly deferred by product decision). Its static or keyed Opacity becomes its group's Opacity and keys whether or not it moves, on the clock of its Motion keys, and import reports the group's pass-through with the export rule at that Opacity; **before**, nondefault Opacity and Opacity keys omitted the nest (synthetic structural tests; no Adobe-native nest-Opacity source or render). Media and nest placements are read by one placement reader, so a nest's master marks annotate its source as for media. Child video Motion, Opacity and Crop use the root mapping, with Crop guides kept inside the group; this adds no loss beyond those mappings. Video transitions linked to a nest are read and reported like those of any clip, and an audio transition linked to a nest's audio item is omitted (audio transition rows). The native writer rejects video transition records at every nested depth, as it does at the root; an inner sound's fades export as audio transitions of its nested sequence. | Rejections convert nothing lossy; synthetic rejection and read-bound tests. A retimed nest's window ends move at most **0.5 ms** each, and its omitted interpolation and Motion keys change the picture unboundedly; synthetic structural and clock tests, no Adobe-native fixture or render. Hidden clips inside a nest become hidden inner layers through JRB-1966's shared mapping, and a hidden nest a hidden group (hidden nest row). |
| Both directions: nested occurrence effects | **Import:** mapped non-Transform effects reuse clip mappers on an identity picture Group after inner/source stacks and before outer Motion/Opacity. Group-relative spatial falloff, tiling and noise coverage are diagnosed approximations. Frame-dependent classes without established Group bounds (Directional Blur, repeated-edge Blur, Mosaic, Find Edges, Lens, Ramp, Replicate and Sharpen), Posterize Time and Film Impact Stroke are omitted with context; active Corner Pin omits the whole occurrence to preserve coverage, while a bypassed pin is omitted individually. Retimed effect keys keep static values with per-property diagnostics. Masks and the separate single-Transform path retain the [nested occurrence effect restrictions](../../crates/premiere_file/README.md#nested-occurrence-effects). **Export:** the existing Group-to-nest route retains the editable picture/effect boundary before outer Motion. Edited-export evidence covers Blur and Brightness & Contrast, not all mapped import effects. | Both directions: expanded nested layers (JRB-1979; historical) | **The limits and budget tests in this row are superseded by the quota cleanup; counts and retained bytes no longer omit content.** Historical behavior: `MAX_EXPANDED_LAYERS = 1024` bounds inline expansion separately from the unchanged 256 placements per sequence. On import, tracks are visited bottom first and nests in timeline order; a placement that would exceed the bound is omitted, not the timeline. Its warning names the native item: `nested sequence would bring its timeline to {total} layers, counting its group and every inline copy; at most 1024 are converted`. Export rejects an oversized timeline. Import also enforces a new per-top-level-read budget of 256 MiB of accounted payload (string lengths and vector element sizes, not allocator overhead or resident memory). Placements are copied only after the overlap rule and layer limit keep them. The budget charges each copy and, before they are kept, the items of every sequence being read (a sequence's name and GUID, each media and graphic item, caption cue and caption track style, and each sound). A copy or item that would pass it is an **Occurrence** omission (a caption style omits its **Track**, and a nested sequence whose name and GUID do not fit omits its placement), and its siblings stay: `timeline read limit reached: one top-level read may read and copy at most {bytes} bytes of timeline data`. Charges follow read order and only grow: a failed read or a dropped item keeps its charge. So projects over the budget (many large nested sequences read but not placed, large repeated copies, or large items) lose later placements or items; projects within it convert unchanged. | Measured structural XML expansion of `corporate_slideshow`: 725 layers, resolving placed `VideoSequenceSource` references recursively and counting each group plus inline copies; not a converter diagnostic (unsupported effects and other placement rules reject those placements first). Maximum video nesting depth across 47 staged cases: 3. Synthetic same-asset 256/512/1024-layer exports took 1.57/1.21/1.53 s on the local Mac, each 60 frames at 1080p30 (2 s); startup included, with no superlinear growth observed. This is bounded-load evidence, not an arbitrary-project performance guarantee. The read budget has synthetic reader tests with small injected budgets (`format::reader::nested::tests`, including `shared_text_of_unfinished_reads_is_charged_before_a_nest_descends`, `each_sound_is_admitted_as_it_is_read` and `items_are_charged_for_their_keys`); no large-allocation run and no Adobe evidence. |
| Premiere → FX: retained timeline data (historical reader limit) | **Removed by the quota cleanup; the following describes historical behavior/evidence, not current admission.** One project load keeps at most 256 MiB of accounted timeline data (strings, items, key vectors and every nested copy) across its selected timelines, charged in GUID order before each is kept. A timeline that would pass it is a **Sequence** omission: `retained timeline limit reached: this timeline holds {bytes} bytes of timeline data; {remaining} of the {limit} bytes that one project load keeps remain`. Earlier timelines and later ones that fit are kept; an explicitly selected timeline is charged alone. Projects whose selected timelines together pass the limit now lose the later over-limit timelines. | No lossy conversion. One synthetic `format::reader::load::tests` test with injected limits. Accounted structural bytes, not allocator overhead or resident memory (RSS). With the per-read budget (the items read and the copies made), the timeline data held at once is at most this 256 MiB, one read's 256 MiB and the one item being decoded. The input XML and its record graph, the media records, the track and transition lists of the read in progress and the omission list are outside these budgets, so they do not bound all memory. No Adobe evidence. |
| Both directions: omission list limit (new) | Each conversion's omission list reports an omission once, in first-report order, and holds at most 1024 omissions and 1 MiB (1048576 bytes) of their record and reason text, with room kept for its summary. Inner stills (IN1 rules), Color Mattes and graphics import inside the group with their own rules. **Export:** a group's `AudioLayer`s export into its nested sequence as top-level sounds do; a nest whose sequence has sound gets one audio item (default Volume, the video item's Enable and ranges) and one `Link` record [video item, audio item], as Premiere 26.5.1 saves them; an item's non-unity gain is import-only (it exports folded into the inner sounds). Export form: the records of the inner I4 item that IN2 export gate Part B reopened and rendered, apart from Start/End and media paths (no new native form). Level-keyed items: `premiere_isolated_nest_audio_outer_keys_26_5` is Premiere 26.5.1's own save of one (keys authored through its scripting API, UI not inspected; `structural_only`, AME render pinned as long-term Asset `eSCZMZ4ckQRGClY36oXP_vid`; [evidence](../../crates/premiere_file/tests/fixtures/nest_audio_outer_keys_proof/README.md)). Measurements of that render are **source facts, not a converter score**: the keys play on the item's In/Out clock (0.0057 dB rms, against 8.93 dB for a clip clock and 3.43 dB for the outer sequence's), Linear segments follow the clip fader law, the inner −6 dB multiplies, the first and last Levels hold beyond the keys, a Hold steps at its key, and the sound stops at End while the inner audio plays on. The public `adobe_nest_audio_item_level_keys_play_its_sound_until_its_end` imports it as one audio layer at 1–7 s from 1.5 s whose keys lie at layer 0.5, 1.5, 2, 2.5, 3.5 and 4.5 s (−6, −18, −18 dB, silent, 0 and −12 dB), beside the picture's group of 1–8 s from 1.5 s without sound, and `a_nest_audio_item_plays_its_sequence_from_in_until_its_end` edits it (an exact span imports as saved; an item longer than its Out − In, at twice the speed, reversed, remapped, empty, from a negative In or past the tick range is omitted with its reason, and a copy of the item that is empty, starts before 0, plays past its Out or passes the tick range is omitted alone beside it). The converter's render of it is **not compared**, so render fidelity stays unmeasured. A keyed inner sound under a keyed item, several items and hidden or disabled pictures have no native save; those rows remain XML edits. Held-Level folding and the Hold product are exact compositions of the read Level keys; each Linear segment that a sound takes keeps the fitted fader curve and its error (clip Volume keys row), and no native fidelity is claimed for either. On XML edits of the fixture (`an_audio_item_with_level_keys_plays_alone_with_them_on_each_sounds_clock`, `item_and_inner_level_keys_combine_where_one_holds_or_both_step_over_the_sound`, `each_audio_item_of_a_nest_plays_alone_with_its_own_level_keys`, `item_level_keys_past_the_tick_range_on_a_sounds_clock_omit_that_sound`, `an_audio_item_whose_gain_overflows_is_omitted_as_it_is_read`, and the public `a_nest_audio_items_level_keys_import_editable_on_its_sound_and_round_trip`, whose edited key survives export and reimport, `a_nest_audio_items_hold_levels_multiply_into_each_keyed_sound_and_round_trip`, whose four sounds of two items keep their own Hold products or item keys, at zero gain under a disabled item, and whose one edited product key survives export and reimport while the other three tracks come back unchanged, `nested_sounds_whose_level_keys_cannot_import_are_kept_silent`, and `a_nest_sound_that_cannot_take_its_items_level_keys_is_omitted_alone`, where I4 or a sibling that plays after it fails on its clock, its Hold product or its gain and the other imports as it does alone) and synthetic (`level_over_is_the_one_level_that_a_source_range_hears`, `a_sound_takes_the_item_level_keys_while_one_level_holds_over_it`, `step_levels_multiply_into_hold_keys_at_every_key_of_either`, which also compares the product at every tick next to a key, through a second nest level, `level_keys_reach_each_nested_sound_on_its_own_clock`). Tests: `an_audio_item_that_no_group_carries_plays_its_sound_alone` (the pinned fixture and nine edits of it, one an item trimmed to In 1 s and Start 11 s), `each_audio_item_of_a_nest_with_several_plays_alone` (a second item on another track, with the picture kept, rejected or absent), `a_sound_that_plays_alone_keeps_every_nested_clock` (synthetic), `every_sound_that_plays_alone_is_kept` (251 items, 257 timed items with the root's video), `a_nest_sound_under_a_hidden_picture_plays_alone_and_round_trips`. Named flips: the range and Enable rows of the former `a_nest_sound_that_cannot_play_as_a_group_plays_is_omitted_with_its_reason` now play alone, and so do its two items for one placement (formerly omitted as ambiguous); its sound-only row is `a_nest_of_only_sound_plays_it_through_its_item_or_is_omitted_with_its_reason`. The keyed Level row of the former `a_nest_sound_with_volume_keys_or_one_channel_is_not_read` (now `a_nest_sound_with_unreadable_volume_keyed_mute_or_one_channel_is_not_read`) now plays alone as the first row of `item_and_inner_level_keys_combine_where_one_holds_or_both_step_over_the_sound`. |
| FX → Premiere: group as a nested sequence (JRB-1979) | Each group becomes a new inner sequence of its children and a placement from inner time zero over the group's range. Copies of one Premiere sequence become separate inner sequences: re-sharing identical groups as one inner sequence is unsupported (deferred product decision). The group name becomes the sequence name; a group description is dropped and reported. Children past the group's range stay in the inner sequence but are hidden by the placement's out point, as they were in the group. A picture-only group longer than its exported children would place past its inner sequence's end, which is unverified in Premiere, so native lowering **omits and reports the group**, preserving its supported siblings and allowing the ordinary CLI to select its editable AE fallback. A longer group containing retained nested audio still rejects rather than silently losing sound. Native reading/writing still rejects an out point past the inner sequence's end. Its placement carries the group's edits (edited group row) and its blend mode (Opacity Blend Mode row). Other than a stage group, which exports as one clip (clip processing stage row), groups with a track matte, a background (static, or keyed padding or corner radius), animation that the placement cannot carry, a time remap, motion blur, a mask that the F17 check rejects, no name, or more than 8 levels are omitted and reported, and media under them is not inspected. A group with a layer that export would place but whose range the sequence grid collapses to zero frames, which no Premiere placement can hold, is omitted before any of it is written and reported with that layer, its range and the rate; a layer that export omits by its own rule first (a still whose fit or sourceRect a clip cannot carry, a track matte source of no exported clip, a stage group that neither one clip nor a nest carries) keeps the group and its own reason, and a nest inside it counts its own layers, so only the smallest such group goes; the media under it is still inspected. The former 256-placement and 1024-expanded-layer policies are removed; depth, timing, supported-property and cycle checks remain. | Boundaries snap as absolute document times on the sequence grid, so each moves at most **half a frame (16.7 ms at 30 fps)**, as at top level. A nest can be transparent over lower content; export accepts its gaps without requiring a black canvas. Synthetic export, writer read-back, and fixture round-trip tests; no Premiere reopen/render check. |
| Either direction: hidden nested sequence (JRB-2077) | Import: a nest placement whose clip Enable is off (`ClipTrackItem/IsMuted`) or that sits on a track whose output is off (`Track/IsMuted`) becomes a **hidden** group, with its content, as a media clip becomes a hidden layer (JRB-1966). **Before**, both were omitted ("disabled nested sequence occurrence not converted", "… on a muted video track not converted"). Export: a hidden group becomes a disabled nest (`IsMuted` on its placement), at any depth; **before**, it was omitted ("hidden groups would become visible"). A hidden stage group stays one disabled clip. | **No new loss.** Adobe-saved fixture `premiere_isolated_hidden_nest_26_5` (Premiere 26.5.1; `video_reference` at the standard policy by user decision 2026-09-27): its AME render, pinned as long-term Asset `bg92kheu3h3QARYWYmWg_vid`, shows neither hidden nest, and a fresh conversion scored against it measures mean 0.999707 / min 0.997769 with every frame from 2 s red in both renders; the pixel test covers appearance and timing of the visible nest and the cut, the structural tests cover that hidden groups and their children survive, and neither replaces the other; the public test imports both as hidden groups and round-trips them through disabled nests. Its three nest audio items now pair with their silent nests and play nothing (IN2); before, each was omitted ("expected AudioMediaSource, found AudioSequenceSource"). Synthetic reader test and a hidden group and stage inside an edited group. Export is Adobe-verified on one document (gate G2, `premiere_export_edited_group`): Premiere 26.5.1 reopened an exported hidden group as a nest placement with Enable off, and AME drew nothing for it. Named flips: `hidden_nests_are_rejected` → `a_disabled_nest_or_one_on_an_output_off_track_reads_as_disabled`; the hidden row of `groups_a_nest_cannot_represent_are_omitted_with_their_reason`. |
| FX → Premiere: edited group as a nested sequence (JRB-2077) | A group with a transform, Opacity, keys, effects or one mask exports as a nest whose placement carries them as a clip does, with the clip's exporters and chain: Motion (position normalized to the canvas, anchor to the nest's canvas-sized picture; skew, 3D and z reported), Opacity, Opacity, Position, Rotation and uniform Scale keys counted from the placement start (F26 applies), the mask as Crop (percentages of the canvas) or a cardinal Linear Wipe whose guide is a child of the group (F17 check; the guide is no content of the nest), then the group's effects in order through the clip effect export (each one it cannot write is omitted and reported, as is a Directional Blur on the group or inside a nest whose placement writes Motion: unsupported Directional Blur variants row), and Enable. Premiere applies the chain from the highest Index down, so the mask applies first, then the effects, as FX applies group masks before group effects. Motion that moves no pixel stays Premiere's default. Group animation that the placement cannot carry whole omits the group, before its children and media are inspected ("group was not exported as a nested sequence: …"): a key on another property (for example Anchor Point, skew or PositionZ), Scale keys that differ between the axes or are unpaired, a track that the key export rejects (for example an Opacity cubic between equal values), or an animation that is not one enabled keyframe track without dependencies. No key is dropped from a placement, so nothing that an animation hides becomes visible. A stage wrapper inside the group stays one inner clip. **Before**, every such group was omitted ("group transforms are not supported", "group animation is not supported", "group effects are not supported", "group masks are not supported"). Reimport keeps an edited nest's Motion, Opacity and their keys, and omits one with effects, a Crop or a Linear Wipe (finding F12). | Values and keys are the clip mapping's (**no new loss** over it); a Gaussian Blur exports as the current Gaussian Blur (current Gaussian Blur export row), consistent with FX's blur in layer pixels under uniform Scale (**inferred**; non-uniform Scale averages in FX). Synthetic export tests (Motion, Opacity and keys; Crop, keyed wipe, static, keyed, bypassed and unmapped effects; hidden children) and a native-record test of the G2 document (chain in Index order Opacity, Motion, blur, Crop; unique ObjectIDs; inner timing). **Export passed a scoped Adobe gate** (G2, `export_gate` in `premiere_export_edited_group`; UI not inspected; `structural_only`, not scored): Premiere 26.5.1 reopened the export of an edited group (moved, Scale 70, Rotation 10, Opacity keys 100 to 40, a Gaussian Blur, a Crop guide, two trimmed children) and a hidden group without a warning and read 52 values back as written; AME rendered the placement's scale (0.700) and angle (within 0.01°), its Opacity ramp, frame-exact inner cuts, the Crop before the blur (a blurred Crop edge) and nothing for the hidden nest. The Tesseract render's blurred edge is 15–18 % narrower than AME's (measured; its cause is inferred, not established). The gate wrote the blur as Legacy Gaussian Blur; the current blur, a Linear Wipe, Motion keys and other effects on a placement are not Adobe-verified. Named flips: `an_animated_group_is_omitted_with_its_animation` → `an_edited_group_exports_its_motion_opacity_and_keys_on_its_placement`; the transform, effects and mask rows of `groups_a_nest_cannot_represent_are_omitted_with_their_reason` (the mask row now names canonical_mask's reason); `media_under_an_omitted_group_is_not_inspected` is a table: AnchorPointX keys, ScaleX keys alone and an Opacity cubic between equal values each omit the group with its reason, keep the other group and leave the omitted group's media uninspected. |
| Premiere → FX: inner media at a nest boundary (JRB-1979) | A constant-rate inner media clip, slow, fast or reversed, trims its source bounds in proportion to its authored source and timeline spans and keeps its editable playback (from #4614). Inner media with source-time remapping is omitted with the outer placement, inner clip, track and time range; unit-speed siblings still convert. A Linear Wipe on the nest placement itself omits that placement. A wipe on a unit-speed inner clip uses the shared video mapping, with its guide parented inside the group just like Crop. Still images, Color Mattes and text inside a nested sequence convert with their own rules in both directions: import keeps them in the nest's group, and export writes each Image, Text or non-guide Rect layer of a group into its inner sequence; what those rules reject is omitted per item with the same context, and the rest of the nest or group converts. Effects on an inner clip use the shared per-layer effect mapping in both directions, with effect ids unique across the document; effects on the nest placement itself still omit it. | Structural-only tests check the retime omission and the inner wipe's distinct layer/mask IDs, group parent, mask wiring and reverse mapping, the still, matte and text children in both directions (`stills_mattes_and_text_inside_a_nest_import_into_its_group`, `stills_and_rectangles_inside_a_group_export_into_its_nest`), and an inner Gaussian Blur imported onto the group child and written in the inner sequence; no new Adobe visual claim. Flat speed/reverse and wipe behavior stays main's. |

### Supported video formats

The accepted set uses existing native `tsrct export` decoders in formats Adobe
documents Premiere importing. H.264 and HEVC also decode through WebCodecs;
ProRes 4444 is native-only, with an import diagnostic. AME has rendered the derived HEVC input; a generated HEVC Premiere
project remains unverified. The existing frame-rate, sequence-canvas and
sourceRect rules apply to admitted codecs. Every other video file rejects with its
file extension or sample-entry code, in both directions.

| Container | Codec (sample entry) | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| MP4 `.mp4`, QuickTime `.mov` | H.264 `avc1` | Accepted: SPS progressive 8-bit 4:2:0; colour BT.709, unspecified or a pass-through declaration (HDR row above) |
| MP4 `.mp4`, QuickTime `.mov` | HEVC Main or Main 10 `hvc1` | Accepted: `hvcC` Main or Main 10 at 8- or 10-bit 4:2:0 with complete VPS/SPS/PPS arrays, one layer; SPS/VUI progressive, square, limited range, BT.709, unspecified or a pass-through colour; a `dvcC`/`dvvC` box only with a plain HEVC base layer; SPS syntax limited as in [HEVC parameter sets](#hevc-parameter-sets) |
| MP4 `.mp4`, QuickTime `.mov` | Dolby Vision HEVC `dvh1`, `dvhe` | Rejected by name until the engine's sample-entry tables map them (Dolby Vision row above): `Dolby Vision dvh1 sample entries are not decodable by the Tesseract engine; hvc1 with a Dolby Vision record converts` |
| MP4 `.mp4`, QuickTime `.mov` | Apple ProRes 422 `apco` `apcs` `apcn` `apch`, 4444 `ap4h` `ap4x` | FX → Premiere: accepted and passed through (profile and 4444 alpha from the sample entry; limited range); Premiere → FX: `ap4h` admitted native-only, other profiles `requires_transcode` (ProRes row above) |
| MP4 `.mp4`, QuickTime `.mov` | `avc3`/`hev1` (in-band parameter sets), 12-bit, 4:2:2 or 4:4:4 HEVC, 10-bit H.264, VP9 `vp09`, AV1 `av01`, MPEG-4 Part 2 `mp4v`, Apple ProRes RAW `aprn` `aprh`, APV, DNxHD/HR, Motion JPEG, other | `video codec "<code>" is unsupported` |
| `.m4v` | any | Rejected by name: `tsrct export` finds `.m4v` video, but `AUDIO_EXTENSIONS` in `crates/resource/src/lib.rs` has no `m4v`, so its audio preflight fails every `.m4v` video layer, even a video-only one, as never resolved. Adding `m4v` there lifts this rejection |
| Any other extension (for example Matroska, WebM, AVI, MXF, MPEG-TS) | any | `video file extension "<ext>" is unsupported`; other container content under an accepted name fails MP4 parsing |

Accepted containers still reject fragmented MP4, a second video or audio
stream, subtitle tracks, non-square pixels, full range, colour codes outside the
pass-through table, and clean-aperture cropping; timecode and other data tracks
are ignored. They now also reject an interlaced QuickTime `fiel` box, for H.264 and
HEVC alike; no approved case has one. A `clap` box that declares the whole
picture with zero offsets, as iPhone captures do, crops nothing and is accepted.
HEVC rejects every VUI
`frame_field_info_present_flag=1`, a deliberate over-rejection: progressive
streams may set it to carry picture-timing SEI, which conversion does not read.
The browser WebCodecs decoder decodes HEVC only where the browser does.

#### HEVC parameter sets

The VUI checks are only as sound as the SPS fields read before them, so the
reader parses only syntax that a captured encoder stream exercises. Each accepted
branch has a captured `hvcC` record and a full-range twin that differs only in
`video_full_range_flag` (`crates/premiere_file/src/tests/video_format.rs`).
The `hvcC` header must declare Main or Main 10 at 8- or 10-bit 4:2:0, and each SPS must declare the
same chroma format and bit depths, because decoders read them from the SPS. The
SPS is also read for the interlaced-source flag, the decoded size and the VUI,
because the sample-entry `colr` and `pasp` boxes are optional: the in-repo
iPhone-shaped HEVC `tests/rendering-custom/fixtures/ios11361_p3_patches.mov`
has neither and declares Display-P3 primaries only in its VUI, which box-only
checks would accept as BT.709.

| SPS syntax before the VUI | Result | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Sub-layers without sub-layer profile or level fields | Accepted | x265 4.3 `temporal-layers=3` capture and twin |
| Sub-layer profile or level fields | Rejected until a capture exists | x265 and VideoToolbox never write them |
| Scaling lists, with or without `scaling_list_data` | Accepted | x265 `scaling-list=<file>` capture and twin (default, copied, and DPCM matrices, DC coefficients); VideoToolbox enables lists without data |
| PCM (`pcm_enabled_flag`) | Rejected until a capture exists | No local encoder writes it |
| Explicitly coded short-term reference picture sets | Accepted | macOS 26.5.1 VideoToolbox capture and twin: four sets of negative pictures; positive entries use the same read |
| Predicted reference picture sets (`inter_ref_pic_set_prediction_flag`) | Rejected until a capture exists | VideoToolbox codes every set explicitly; x265 codes none in the SPS |
| Long-term reference pictures (`long_term_ref_pics_present_flag`) | Rejected until a capture exists | No local encoder writes them |

The rejection tests set each flag in a captured record at the bit position that
FFmpeg's `trace_headers` reports.

#### Renderer check

The renderer-check numbers come from this comparison. Both sides are relabelled
BT.709 limited range without converting pixels and paired by frame index; frames
1–60 of `ssim.log` are H.264 and 61–120 HEVC.

```sh
F=crates/premiere_file/tests/fixtures
TAGS="format=yuv420p,setparams=color_primaries=bt709:color_trc=bt709:colorspace=bt709:range=tv"
target/debug/tsrct-conv convert $F/feature_video_formats_strict.prproj --to tesseract \
  --sequence c8acf9c1-34b2-4086-9f55-d528950a7059 --output /tmp/formats
target/debug/tsrct export --project /tmp/formats/project.tsrct --output /tmp/formats.mp4
ffmpeg -i /tmp/formats.mp4 -i $F/video-30fps-10s.mp4 -i $F/feature_video_formats_hevc.mp4 \
  -filter_complex "[1:v]trim=2:4,setpts=PTS-STARTPTS,$TAGS[a];[2:v]trim=0:2,setpts=PTS-STARTPTS,$TAGS[b];\
[a][b]concat=n=2:v=1:a=0,setpts=N/30/TB[reference];[0:v]$TAGS,setpts=N/30/TB[export];\
[export][reference]ssim=stats_file=ssim.log" -f null -
```

### Review notes

- Audio placements read their absolute In/Out; audio master-range handling awaits an audio subclip fixture.
- Graphic placements use the same occurrence-level nonzero/invalid `OriginalSubClipTimeOffset` rejection as video and audio (synthetic pinned-text regression); no graphic master-range support is added.
- Eye Contact replacement audio.

## Tests

`tests/cli_smoke.rs` checks arguments, command names, success messages, standard
error, and exit codes by running the executable. Conversion rules,
media validation, publication, and round trips are tested through the public
library API in `crates/premiere_file/tests/conversion`.

From the conv workspace (`opensource/conv` in the enclosing repository):

```sh
make test
make check
make clippy
make fmt
```

The portable workspace contains exactly the eight packages listed in the root
`Cargo.toml`: `tsrct-conv`, `media_transcode`, `fx_conv`, `premiere_file`,
`aftereffects_file`, `tesseract_file`, `fx_schema`, and `fx_keyframe_bake`; it
uses no patched dependencies. The workspace
builds directly without staging, a second repository or private Cargo configuration.

Build-profile parity matters: the existing XML-depth boundary test can overflow
a test thread's stack with unoptimized parser dependencies. The workspace keeps dependency `opt-level = 2`; it does not raise stack
limits or relax the XML depth limit. Passing tests under this declared profile
does not establish safety under every unoptimized build.

For every new conversion feature, follow
the independent fixture and video-proof workflow described below:
add a minimal single-feature Premiere project, an independently exported and
SHA-verified MP4, and a scored FX render. Set `proof: video_reference`
only after the comparison satisfies the standard score policy.
The enclosing repository's `make check-prproj-score` integration target
strictly scores every `video_reference` case in `tests/manifest.json` (and fails
if none exist) against the independently exported MP4s checked in under
`tests/references/premiere/`; no remote Asset download or authenticated resolver
is involved. The [corpus proof contract](../../tests/README.md#evidence-contract)
is the source of truth for the current case list and score policy. The former
`structural_only` real-project corpus is no longer registered or distributed as
test coverage.

The original unit-test media fixture in `crates/premiere_file/tests/fixtures`
is one second of video-only H.264 at 1080p30. The focused Adobe-oracle fixtures
also include small 10-second colored/timecoded 1080p30 sources. Their QuickTime
MOV source is stream-copy remuxed (`ffmpeg -map 0:v:0 -c copy -f mov`), not a
renamed MP4. A non-square variant exercises rejection (`setsar=2/1`) and an
HDR-tagged one the colour pass-through (BT.2020/PQ declarations); they are
synthetic fixtures, not Adobe references. `feature_hdr_hlg_hvc1.mov` is a
320×180 VideoToolbox Main 10 HLG encode with a timecode track (`-c:v
hevc_videotoolbox -profile:v main10 -pix_fmt p010le -color_primaries bt2020
-color_trc arib-std-b67 -colorspace bt2020nc -tag:v hvc1 -timecode 00:00:00:00
-f mov` on a `setparams`-tagged source).
Tests also mutate container pixel-aspect and edit-list metadata.
Six-frame samples cover the other seven supported rates. Their offline generation
command is recorded in `crates/premiere_file/src/tests/media.rs`. The HEVC Main
and VP9 samples and the captured x265 and VideoToolbox parameter-set variants,
the Main 10 HLG and PQ records among them, are recorded in
`crates/premiere_file/src/tests/video_format.rs`.

### Linked AE Twirl: editable approximation in both directions

Standalone static Shape/Text import without Layer Styles transports the Twirl
image with a late editable CornerPin. Full-span identity-own-clock Group export
stages transformed content inside a full-frame native carrier. Linked pictures,
styles, other clocks/planes/ancestors and kernel/edge behavior retain diagnosed
editable approximations; supplied user FX is never undone to guess a source plane.
Independent native/render proof remains pending.
See the [canonical AE support and proof limits](../../docs/after-effects-support.md#twirl-owner-and-frame-planes--both-direction-editable-approximation).


Premiere-native Invert Alpha15 has a bounded editable picture/matte import.
Current full-span still edits export through native nested sequences/Track Matte
Key; unchanged trimmed-video graph clocks are tested through the linked-AEP
package route, not current linked-video edits/bypass. Bypassed Alpha with a
retained effect mask is omitted without losing its picture; active masked-video
admission retains inherited occurrence-rejection limits. This does
not add a standalone AEP import feature. See the Premiere crate's
[Invert Alpha scope and limitations](../../crates/premiere_file/README.md#premiere-invert-alpha-bounded-editable-replacement).
Edited-output Adobe acceptance and RGB/alpha fidelity remain unmeasured.
